<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem.compl}
Let $P$ be a Noetherian ring, $I\subset P$ an ideal and 
$M$ a $P$-module. Let
${}^{\wedge}$ denote the $I$-adic completion.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $M^{\wedge}$ depends only on the radical of $I$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $M^{\wedge}$ is $I$-adically complete.
\@par\hangindent 2em $I^m M^\wedge = (I^m M)^\wedge$ and $M/I^m M=M^{\wedge}/I^m M^{\wedge}$ for all $m \ge 1$.
\@par\hangindent 2em If $P$ is $I$-adically complete then $I\subset \operatorname{rad} (P)$.\footnote{$\operatorname{rad} (P)$ denotes the Jacobson radical of $P$.}\\
In particular, $IP^\wedge \subset \operatorname{rad}(P^\wedge)$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $P^{\wedge}$ is Noetherian and  $P\to P^{\wedge}$ is flat.
\@par\hangindent 2em If  $M$ is a finite $P$-module, then $M^\wedge = M\otimes_P P^\wedge$. \\
In particular, if $J\subset P$ is an ideal, then  $(P/J)^\wedge = P^\wedge / JP^\wedge$.
\@par\hangindent 2em The completion is an exact functor on the caetgory of finite $P$-modules.
\@par\hangindent 2em  Let $J\subset P$ be an ideal. If $P$ is
$I$-adically complete and $P/I$ is $J$-adically complete, then $P$ is $J$-adically complete.
\@par\hangindent 2em Let  $M$ be a finite $P$-module. The kernel 
$\cap_{n=1}^\infty I^nM$ of $M \to M^\wedge$ consists of those $x \in M$ annihilated by some element of $1 + I$. In particular, 
since $(M^\wedge)^\wedge = M^\wedge$,
$\cap_{n=1}^\infty I^nM^\wedge =0$.
\@par\hangindent 2em Let  $I\subset \operatorname{rad}(P)$. If $M$ is finite over $P$ then $M\to M^\wedge$ is injective. In particular,  $P\to P^\wedge$  is injective
and $ P^\wedge$ is faithfully flat over $P$.
Furthermore, if  $IM =M$ then $M=0$ (Nakayama's lemma).
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $J\subset P$ be an ideal and $M$ a finite $P$-module, then $(M^{\wedge,I})^{\wedge, J} = M^{\wedge, I+J}$. \@par\hangindent 2em If $I=\frak{m}$ is a maximal ideal then $P^{\wedge} = (P_\frak{m})^{\wedge,\frak{m}}$ is a local ring with maximal ideal $\frak{m} P^{\wedge}$ and residue field $P^{\wedge}/\frak{m} P^{\wedge} = P/\frak{m}.$ If $P$ is regular then $P^\wedge$ is regular.
\@par\hangindent 2em If $(P,\frak{m})$ is local, then $P$ is regular iff its $\frak{m}$-adic completion $P^{\wedge}$ is regular.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
1. follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.95.9, tag 0319]{Sta19}, 2. and 3. from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.95.3, tag 05GG]{Sta19}, 4. from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.95.6, tag 05GI]{Sta19}. 5. follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.96.6, tag 0316 and Lemma 10.96.2, tag 00MB]{Sta19}, 6. and 7.  from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.96.1, tag 00MA]{Sta19} and 8. from 
\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.96.10, tag 0DYC]{Sta19}.
9. follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 10.17]{AM69},  and
10. from  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Corollary 10.19]{AM69}
and \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 8.14 and Theorem 2.2]{Mat86}.
For 11. see  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Section 10, Exercise 5]{AM69}.

For 12. let $\frak{m}$ be maximal. Then $P_\frak{m}/(\frak{m} P_\frak{m})^n =(P/\frak{m}^n)_\frak{m}
= P/\frak{m}^n$ since $P/\frak{m}^n$ is already local with maximal ideal 
$\frak{m}/\frak{m}^n$. Hence $(P_\frak{m})^\wedge =P^\wedge$ is local and the residue fields satisfy 
$(P_\frak{m})^\wedge/\frak{m}(P_\frak{m})^\wedge =P_\frak{m}/\frak{m} P_\frak{m} =  \operatorname{Quot} (P/\frak{m}) = P/\frak{m} = P^\wedge/\frak{m} P^\wedge$. 
The regularity of $P^\wedge$ follows from 13..

For 13. recall that a Noetherian local ring $(P,\frak{m})$ with residue field $k$ is regular if its embedding dimension $\dim_{k}\frak{m}/\frak{m}^2$ equals its dimension $\dim P$  (cf. Definition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@def.sing\endcsname\@firstoftwo{def.sing}}}). It follows that $P$ is regular iﬀ its completion is regular, since the dimension and embedding dimension remain the same after
completion.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{rm.compl}
{\em Although we are mainly interested in Noetherian rings, we like to mention that some of the above properties 1. - 12. hold more generally. Let $P$ be any ring and $M$ a $P$-module.\\
1. holds without any further assumption (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.95.9, tag 0319]{Sta19}).\\
2. and 3. hold if the ideal $I\subset P$ is finitely generated ( \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.95.3, tag 05GG]{Sta19}).  4. generalizes to $IR^\wedge \subset \operatorname{rad}(P^\wedge)$ for any ideal $I$ by \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.95.6, tag 05GI]{Sta19}.\\
The following lemma is wrong if $P$ is not Noetherian.  The canonical map $M\otimes_PN^\wedge\to (M\otimes_PN)^\wedge$ is always surjective but in general not injective. For a counterexample see \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 05JD]{Sta19}.}
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem.lim}
Let $P$ be Noetherian, $I\subset P$ an ideal and ${}^\wedge$ the $I$-adic completion. Then
$$(M\otimes_PN)^\wedge = M^\wedge \otimes_PN = M\otimes_PN^\wedge$$
for finitely generated $P$-modules $M$ and $N$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6., since  $M\otimes_PN$ is finite over $P$,
$(M\otimes_PN)^\wedge 
= (M\otimes_PN)\otimes_PP^\wedge = (M\otimes_PP^\wedge)\otimes_PN=  M\otimes_P(N\otimes_PP^\wedge)$. Now apply 
 Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6. again.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\em{In our applications the fiber $M(\frak{p})$ is not the right object to consider if $\frak{p}$ is not a maximal ideal. For example, let $A=K[t]$, $K$ a field, $M=R=A[[x]]=A[x]^{\wedge,\langle x \rangle}$ and $\frak{p}=\langle 0\rangle$. Then the fiber $R(\frak{p}) = A[[x]](\langle 0\rangle)=(K[t])[[x]] \otimes_{K[t]}K(t)$ is  neither local nor complete, while we are interested in the complete local ring $A(\langle 0\rangle)[[x]]= K(t)[[x]]$, which is the $\langle x \rangle$-adic completion of $A[[x]](\langle 0\rangle)$. That is, the right object to consider is not the fiber $M(\frak{p})$ but the completed fiber
(in this case $M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\langle x \rangle}$), which we define now.}
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def.cf} Let  $A  \to R$ ba a ring map, $I\subset R$ an ideal, $M$ an $R$-module,
and $\frak{p}$ a prime ideal   of $A$. We define
$$
\begin{array}{lll}
\hat R_\frak{p} &:= (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I},  & \hat R(\frak{p}) := R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}\\
\hat M_\frak{p} & :=(M_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} &
\hat M(\frak{p}):= M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}  
\end{array}
$$
and call $\hat M_\frak{p}$  the {\em completed stalk} of $M$ over $\frak{p}$ and 
$\hat M(\frak{p})$ the {\em completed fiber} of $M$ over $\frak{p}$.         
$\hat M_\frak{p}$ is an  $\hat R_\frak{p}$-module and 
$\hat M(\frak{p})$ an $\hat R(\frak{p})$-module, which is  also a $k(\frak{p}) $-vector space. We call 
$$\hat d_{\frak{p}} (M):= \dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})$$
{\em the completed fiber dimension of $M$ over} $\frak{p}$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def.ctp}
Let  $A \to R$ be a ring map, $I\subset R$ an ideal, and  $B$ an $A$-algebra . 
We define  the {\em ($I$-adic) completed tensor product} of $R$  and $B$ over $A$ as the ring
$$R \hat\otimes_A B :=  \lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\big((R/I^mR) \otimes_A B \big).$$
If  $M$ is an $R$-module  and $N$ a $B$-module, we define the {\em ($I$-adic) completed tensor product} of  $M$  and $N$ over $A$ as the $R \hat\otimes_A B$-module 
$$M \hat\otimes_A N :=  \lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\big((M/I^m M)\otimes_A N\big).$$
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop.pctp} Let  $A \to R$ be a ring map,
$I\subset R$ an ideal, $B$ an $A$-algebra, $M$ an $R$-module, and $N$ a $B$-module.
The $I$-adic completed tensor product has the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em
$R \hat\otimes_A B = (R \otimes_A B)^{\wedge}$,
$M \hat\otimes_A N = (M \otimes_A N)^{\wedge}$.\\
 If $I$ is finitely generated, then 
$M \hat\otimes_A N= M^\wedge  \hat\otimes_A N$.
In particular,
$$ M\hat \otimes_A A_\frak{p} =(M^\wedge)\hat\otimes_A A_\frak{p}, \quad  M\hat \otimes_A k(\frak{p}) =(M^\wedge)\hat\otimes_A k(\frak{p}).$$
\@par\hangindent 2em 
Let $A$, $B$ and $R$ be Noetherian. If $M$ is finitely generated over $R$ and $N$ is finitely genrated over $B$, then
$$
\begin{array}{lll}
M \hat\otimes_A N &\cong (M  \otimes_A N) \otimes_{R\otimes_A B}(R\otimes_A B)^{\wedge,IR \otimes_A B}\\
&= (M  \otimes_A N) \otimes_{R\otimes_A B}(R\hat\otimes_A B).
\end{array}
$$  
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $A$ and $R$ be Noetherian and $I \subset \operatorname{rad}(R)$. 
Then the canonical map
 $M \otimes_A N \to M \hat\otimes_A N$  is injective if $M$ is finite over $R$ and $N$ finite over $A$.
\medskip
\@par\hangindent 2em If $I^m M =0$ for some integer $m$  then
$M \hat\otimes_A N = M \otimes_A N$ for every $A$-module $N$. 
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{ex.pctp}{\em
In particular, let $A$, $B$ be Noetherian, $R=A[x]/J$, $J\subset A[x]$ an ideal, and  $I\subset R$ an ideal. Then  statement 1. and Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6. imply ($^\wedge$ the $I$-adic completion):
$$(A[x]/J)\hat \otimes_AB =((A[x]/J) \otimes_AB)^\wedge=(B[x]/JB[x])^{\wedge}=B[x]^\wedge/JB[x]^{\wedge}.$$
E.g.  for  $R=A[x]$ and $B=k(\frak{p})$ we get $A[x]\hat \otimes_A k(\frak{p})=k(\frak{p})[x]^{\wedge}$ and if $I = \langle x_1,\ldots,x_n\rangle$,
then $A[[x]]\hat \otimes_AB=B[[x]]$ and $A[[x]]\hat \otimes_Ak(\frak{p})=k(\frak{p})[[x]]$.}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
1. The equality  $M \hat\otimes_A N = (M \otimes_AN)^{\wedge}$ follows from  
$$(M/I^mM)\otimes_AN = (M\otimes_A N)/I^m ({M\otimes_A N}).$$
Moreover, since $M/I^m M = M^\wedge/I^m M^\wedge$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}} and Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.compl}}}
 we have also
$M^\wedge  \hat\otimes_A N =\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\big((M^\wedge/I^m M^\wedge)\otimes_A N\big) = M\hat\otimes_A N.$

2. Since $M$ is  finitely generated over $R$ and $N$ is finitely generated over $B$, 
$M\otimes_A N$ is finitely generated over the Noetherian ring $R\otimes_A B$.
Using item 1,  
Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6. gives the isomorphism.

3.  If
$M$ is finitely generated over $R$ and $N$ is finitely generated over $A$ then $M\otimes_A N$ is finitely generated over $R =R\otimes_A A$. 
The injectivity follows from 1., from 2. (with $B=A$), and from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.10.

4.  Follows from the  definition of the completed tensor product.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor.cpl} 
We have:
 \begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em[(i)] 
 $R\hat\otimes_A A = R^\wedge$, $M\hat\otimes_A A =M^{\wedge}$.
 \medskip
 \@par\hangindent 2em[(ii)]  $\hat M_\frak{p} =M\hat\otimes_A A_\frak{p}$ and $\hat M(\frak{p}) = M\hat\otimes_A k(\frak{p})$ for $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. 
\medskip
 \@par\hangindent 2em [(iii)] If $S$ be multiplicatively closed in $A$, then 
 $M\hat\otimes_A (S^{-1}A) = (S^{-1}M)^{\wedge,IS^{-1}R}$.
 \medskip
 \@par\hangindent 2em[(iv)] If $R$ is $I$-adically complete, then $M$ is I-adically complete. 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.pctp\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.pctp}}}.1  implies (i) and (ii) with $B=A$ and $N=A_\frak{p}$ resp. $N=k(\frak{p}) = A_\frak{p}/\frak{p}$ by definition of 
$\hat M_\frak{p}$ and $\hat M(\frak{p})$, while  
(iii)  follows from  Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.pctp\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.pctp}}}.2. with $N=B=S^{-1}A$, and (iv) follows from  Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6..
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor.rex}
The completed tensor product is right-exact on the category of finitely generated modules. That is, let  
$$
\begin{array}{*{20}l}
M' \to M \to  M'' \to 0 ,  \text{resp.}\\
N' \to N \to N'' \to 0
\end{array}
$$
be exact sequences with $M', M, M''$ finitely generated  $R$-modules and $N', N, N''$ finitely generated   $B$-modules. Then the sequences of finitely generated  $R \hat\otimes_A B$-modules
$$
\begin{array}{*{20}l}
M' \hat\otimes_A N\to M\hat\otimes_A N\to M'' \hat\otimes_A N\to 0,\text{resp.}\\
M \hat\otimes_A N'\to M\hat\otimes_A N\to M\hat\otimes_A N''\to 0\\
\end{array}
$$
are exact.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The sequences 
 $ M'\otimes_A N \to M\otimes_A N\to M'' \otimes_A N\to 0$ and 
  $ M\otimes_A N' \to M\otimes_A N\to M\otimes_A N''\to 0$ 
are exact. Now tensor these sequences with $R\hat\otimes_A B$ over 
$R\otimes_A B$  and apply Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.pctp\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.pctp}}}.2.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor.maxA} We have:
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em [(1)]  $\hat M(\frak{p})= \hat M_\frak{p} /\frak{p} \hat M_\frak{p}$. 
 In particular
$\hat R(\frak{p})= \hat R_\frak{p} /\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p}$.\\
If $I \subset \frak{p} R_\frak{p}$ then $\hat R(\frak{p}) =  R(\frak{p})$.
\@par\hangindent 2em [(2)]  Let $\frak{p}$ be a maximal ideal.  Then
 $$ \hat M(\frak{p}) =  M(\frak{p})^\wedge = M^\wedge (\frak{p}) =M^\wedge/\frak{p} M^\wedge.$$ In particular, if $R$ is complete, then  $ \hat M(\frak{p}) = M(\frak{p})$
and hence  $\hat d_\frak{p} (M) = d_\frak{p} (M). $
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
We apply $M \hat\otimes_A -$ to the exact sequence  $\frak{p} A_\frak{p} \to A_\frak{p} \to k(\frak{p}) \to 0$  of finitely generated  $A_\frak{p}$-modules ($A_\frak{p}$ is Noetherian since $A$ is). 
Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.rex\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.rex}}} (second sequence with $N=B=A_\frak{p}$) and Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.cpl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.cpl}}} (ii) gives the first line of (1).
If $I \subset \frak{p}$ then $IR_\frak{p} \subset \frak{p} R_\frak{p}$ and 
$\hat R_\frak{p} /\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p} = \hat R_\frak{p} /\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p} + I \hat R_\frak{p} = (\hat R_\frak{p} / I \hat R_\frak{p})/\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p} (\hat R_\frak{p} / I \hat R_\frak{p})= R_\frak{p} / I R_\frak{p} +\frak{p} R_\frak{p} =R(\frak{p}).$

(2) If $\frak{p}$ is maximal, $k(\frak{p}) = A/\frak{p}$ and 
Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.rex\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.rex}}} (with $N=B =A$) implies
 $\hat M(\frak{p}) = M \hat \otimes_A A/\frak{p}  = (M \hat\otimes_A A)/ \frak{p} (M \hat\otimes_A A) = M^\wedge/\frak{p} M^\wedge=M^\wedge(\frak{p}).$ 
If $R$ is complete, then $M^\wedge=M$ by Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.cpl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.cpl}}} (iv) and hence $M^\wedge(\frak{p})=M(\frak{p})$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor.pres}
Let  $ R^p \xrightarrow{\text {$T$}} R^q \to  M \to 0$ be an  $R$-presentation of $M$.
Then 
$$ (R \hat\otimes_A B)^p \xrightarrow{\text {$T$}} (R \hat\otimes_A B)^q \to  M \hat\otimes_A B\to 0$$
 is an  $R \hat\otimes_A B$-presentation of $M\hat\otimes_A B$.
 In particular,
$\hat M(\frak{p}) = \operatorname{coker}(\hat R(\frak{p})^p \xrightarrow{\text {$T(\frak{p})$}}\hat R(\frak{p})^q)$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{cor.fitt}
Let $A \to R$ be a ring map and
$$ R^p \xrightarrow{\text {$T$}} R^q \to M \to 0$$
a presentation of the  $R$-module $M$.  The 
ideal generated by all $q-j$ minors of $T$ is denoted by $F_j(M)$ and called the {\em j-th  Fitting ideal} of M. Then, for any ring map $A \to B$,
$$ F_j(M \hat\otimes_A B) =  F_j(M) \cdot R\hat\otimes_A B. $$
In particular, for $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$  and $B = k(\frak{p})$ we get
$ F_j(\hat M(\frak{p})) =  F_j(M) \cdot \hat R(\frak{p})$.
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
This follows since $ (R \hat\otimes_A B)^p \xrightarrow{\text {$T$}} (R \hat\otimes_A B)^q \to M \hat\otimes_A B \to 0$ is a presentation of $M \hat\otimes_A B$ by Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.pres\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.pres}}}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem.sem0}
Let $A\to S$ be a finite morphism of  Noetherian  rings and $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. 
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em The fiber $S(\frak{p})$ is (if non-zero) an Artinian semi-local ring with maximal ideals, say, $\bar\frak{m}_1,..., \bar\frak{m}_r$, and  the (finite) direct sum of its localizations at $\bar\frak{m}_i$. 
\@par\hangindent 2em The stalk $S_\frak{p}$ is a semi-local ring with $\frak{m}_i = \bar\frak{m}_i \cap S_\frak{p}$, $i=1,...,r$,   the maximal ideals of $S_\frak{p}$. We have $\frak{m}_i = \tilde \frak{m}_i S_\frak{p}$, where  $\tilde \frak{m}_i = \frak{m}_i \cap S$ is a prime ideal  of $S$  satisfying  $\tilde \frak{m}_i  \cap A = \frak{m}_i  \cap A = \frak{p}$.\\
Moreover, the Jacobson radical is $\operatorname{rad}(S_\frak{p})=\sqrt{\frak{p} S_\frak{p}} = \frak{m}_1\cap...\cap \frak{m}_r$.
Note that $S_\frak{m} = S$ if $(A,\frak{m})$ is local.
\@par\hangindent 2em If $\frak{p}$ is a {\em maximal} ideal of $A$, then the preimages $\tilde \frak{m}_i = \bar\frak{m}_i \cap S$ are maximal ideals of $S$\,\footnote{This is wrong if $\frak{p}$ is not maximal: Consider $A=\mathbb Z[x]$, $R=A[[y]]$, $I=\langle y\rangle$ and $\frak{p}=\langle p\rangle$ for some prime $p\in \mathbb Z$. The ideal $\frak{m}=\langle p,y\rangle$ is maximal in $R_\frak{p}$. The induced ideal in $R$ is $\langle p,y\rangle$, which is not maximal.}, and these are all maximal ideals of $S$ that lie over $\frak{p}$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
1. $S(\frak{p})$ is finite dimensional over the field $k(\frak{p})$ and hence Artinian (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[00J6]{Sta19}), it has only finitely many maximal ideals (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[00J7]{Sta19}), say 
$\bar\frak{m}_1,...,\bar\frak{m}_r$.  By  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[00JB]{Sta19} any prime ideal is a maximal ideal and $S(\frak{p})$ is semi-local and the finite direct sum of the localizations at its maximal ideals.

2. Since the $\frak{m}_i$ are the preimages in $S_\frak{p}$ of the 
$\bar\frak{m}_i \in S_\frak{p}/\frak{p} S_\frak{p}$,
the  prime ideals $\frak{m}_i$ are in fact maximal ideals, since
 $\frak{m}_i$  contains $\frak{p} S_\frak{p}$ and hence
$S_\frak{p}/\frak{m}_i = S_\frak{p}/\frak{m}_i +\frak{p} S_\frak{p}=(S_\frak{p}/\frak{p} S_\frak{p})/\bar \frak{m}_i$ is a field. Thus $\frak{m}_i$
is a maximal ideal.

Now let  $\frak{m}$ be any maximal ideal of $S_\frak{p}$. We claim $ \frak{m} \cap A_\frak{p}=\frak{p} A_\frak{p}$. In fact,
$\frak{m} \cap A_\frak{p}$ is a prime ideal of $A_\frak{p}$ of the form $\frak{q} A_\frak{p}$ with $\frak{q}\subset \frak{p}$ a prime ideal in $A$ (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 4.1]{Mat86}). Then 
$ \frak{m} \cap A_\frak{p}=\frak{q} A_\frak{p} \subset \frak{p} A_\frak{p}$. Since $A_\frak{p} \to S_\frak{p}$ is finite, there exists (by going-up, \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 9.4]{Mat86}) a prime ideal $\frak{m}'$ of $S_\frak{p}$, $\frak{m}\subset \frak{m}'$ s.t. $\frak{m}' \cap A_\frak{p} = \frak{p} A_\frak{p}$.
Since $\frak{m}$ is maximal, $\frak{m}' = \frak{m}$ and the preimage of 
$\frak{m}$ in $A_\frak{p}$ is
$ \frak{m} \cap A_\frak{p}=\frak{p} A_\frak{p}$. Therefore, the ideal in $S_\frak{p}$ generated by the image of  $\frak{p} A_\frak{p}$, which is 
$\frak{p} S_\frak{p}$, is contained in $\frak{m}$.
Then $\bar \frak{m} = \frak{m}/\frak{p} S_\frak{p}$ is a maximal ideal of $S(\frak{p})$ and hence one of the $\bar\frak{m}_i$ by 1.. It follows that $\frak{m}$ is one of the $\frak{m}_i$ and that $S_\frak{p}$ is semi-local.

We show now that $\frak{m}_i = \tilde \frak{m}_i S_\frak{p}$. Since $\frak{m}_i$ contains $\frak{p} S_\frak{p}$,
the preimage $\tilde \frak{m}_i \subset S$ of $\frak{m}_i$ 
is a prime ideal that contains $\frak{p}$ and hence $\tilde \frak{m}_i S_\frak{p}$ contains $\frak{p} S_\frak{p}$. By \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 4.1]{Mat86}
$\tilde \frak{m}_i S_\frak{p}$ is a prime ideal of $S_\frak{p}$ and it follows that  $S_\frak{p}/\tilde \frak{m}_i S_\frak{p}=S(\frak{p})/\tilde \frak{m}_i S(\frak{p})$ is integral. This hows that $\tilde \frak{m}_i S(\frak{p})$ is a prime ideal of $S(\frak{p})$ and hence a maximal ideal by 1.. By the reasoning above $\tilde \frak{m}_i S_\frak{p} =\frak{m}_i$.


                               

With $S(\frak{p})$ also its reduction $S_\frak{p}/K$, 
$K=\sqrt{\frak{p} S_\frak{p}}$, is semi-local  with the same maximal ideals.
We have 
$K=\frak{m}_1\cap\ldots\cap \frak{m}_r,$
 since $K$ is the preimage in $S_\frak{p}$ of 
$\bar\frak{m}_1\cap\ldots\cap \bar\frak{m}_r = \langle 0\rangle$ (the nilradical of $S_\frak{p}/K$, which is 0). \\
                                
\indent 
3. If $\frak{p}$ is maximal, then $S(\frak{p})= S/\frak{p} S$. Let $\tilde\frak{m}$ be a maximal ideal of $S$ lying over $\frak{p}$. Then $\frak{p} = A\cap \tilde\frak{m}$, hence $\frak{p} S \subset \tilde\frak{m}$ and
$\bar \frak{m} = \tilde \frak{m}/\frak{p} S$ is a maximal ideal of $S(\frak{p})$. Therefore $\tilde\frak{m}$ is one of the $\tilde\frak{m}_i$ as above.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem.sem}
Let $S$ be a semi-local ring with maximal ideals $\frak{m}_1, \ldots ,\frak{m}_r$ and let $I=\operatorname{rad}(S)=\frak{m}_1\cap\ldots\cap \frak{m}_r$ (the Jacobson radical of $S$). Then 
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $I=\frak{m}_1\cdot\ldots\cdot \frak{m}_r$ and the $I$-adic completion $S^{\wedge,I}$ is a direct sum of complete local rings 
$(S_{\frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}\cong (S^{\wedge,I})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}= S^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}\cong (S^{\wedge,I})_{\frak{m}_i} $,
$$S^{\wedge,I} \cong  (S_{\frak{m}_1})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_1}\oplus...\oplus (S_{\frak{m}_r})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_r}.$$
\@par\hangindent 2em $S/I$ is semi-local and a direct sum of fields,
$$S/I \cong S/\frak{m}_1\oplus...\oplus S/\frak{m}_r \cong S_{\frak{m}_1}/IS_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus S_{\frak{m}_r}/IS_{\frak{m}_r}.$$
\@par\hangindent 2em If $S$ is Henselian with respect to $I$,
then $S\cong S_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus S_{\frak{m}_r}$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Statement 1. follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 1.3 and Theorem 8.15]{Mat86}. Since $I \subset \frak{m}_i$ we have $(S^{\wedge,I})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}= S^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}$.
\\
2. $S/I$ is semi-local and isomorphic to $S/\frak{m}_1\oplus...\oplus S/\frak{m}_r$ by \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 1.3 and Theorem 1.4]{Mat86},
and hence an Artinian ring. This implies by \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.52.6, tag 00JB]{Sta19}, that 
$$S/I\cong S_{\frak{m}_1}/IS_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus S_{\frak{m}_r}/IS_{\frak{m}_r}.$$
3. If $S$ is Henselian with respect to $I$ this isomorphism lifts to 
$$S\cong S_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus S_{\frak{m}_r} $$
(\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname{Sta19} Lemma  15.11.6 (2), tag 09XI or \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname{KPR75} Satz 2.6.1 (3)).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem.sem2}
Let $S$ be a ring,  $\frak{m}_1,...,\frak{m}_r$ distinct maximal ideals of $S$, and $I=\frak{m}_1\cap ...\cap\frak{m}_r$. Then 
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $I=\frak{m}_1\cdot...\cdot\frak{m}_r$ and
$S^{\wedge,I}$ is a semi-local ring with maximal ideals 
$\tilde \frak{m}_i :=\frak{m}_i S^{\wedge,I}$, $i=1,...,r$, Jacobson radical $\operatorname{rad}(S^{\wedge,I}) = I S^{\wedge,I} = \tilde \frak{m}_1\cap ...\cap\tilde\frak{m}_r$, 
and
$$ S^{\wedge,I} \cong  S^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_1}} \oplus ...\oplus S^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_r}}.$$
For each $i$, $S^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}} =(S^{\wedge,I})^{\wedge,\tilde\frak{m}_i}\cong (S^{\wedge,I})_{\tilde\frak{m}_i} \cong  (S_{\frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}}$ is a complete local ring with maximal ideal $\frak{m}_i S^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}$ and residue field $S^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}/ \frak{m}_i S^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}= S/\frak{m}_i$.
Moreover, $S^{\wedge,I}/ IS^{\wedge,I}= S/I = S/\frak{m}_1 \oplus...\oplus S/\frak{m}_r$. 

\@par\hangindent 2em If $S$ is Noetherian and $M$ a finite  $S$-module, then $(M^{\wedge,I})_{\frak{m}_i} \cong  M^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}}= (M_{\frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}}$ and
$$ M^{\wedge,I} \cong  M^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_1}} \oplus ...\oplus M^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_r}}.$$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
1. Write $S^{\wedge} =S^{\wedge,I}$. 
Since $IS^\wedge \subset \frak{m}_i S^\wedge$,
$$S/\frak{m}_i = (S/I)/\frak{m}_i =
(S^{\wedge} /I S^\wedge)/\frak{m}_i S^\wedge = S^\wedge /\tilde\frak{m}_i$$ 
is a field, $\tilde\frak{m}_i$ is a maximal ideal of $S^\wedge$. 
Since the $\frak{m}_i$ are pairwise coprime, $I=\frak{m}_1\cdot...\cdot\frak{m}_r$ (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 1.3]{Mat86}) we have
$IS^{\wedge} = \frak{m}_1S^{\wedge} \cdot ... \cdot  \frak{m}_rS^{\wedge} = \tilde\frak{m}_1\cdot...\cdot \tilde\frak{m}_r
= \tilde\frak{m}_1\cap...\cap \tilde\frak{m}_r$. 
By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.4. we have
$IS^{\wedge} \subset \operatorname{rad}(S^{\wedge})$. Since obviously  
$\operatorname{rad}(S^{\wedge})\subset IS^{\wedge}$, we get equality.
This shows that $S^{\wedge}$ is semi-local with maximal ideals $\tilde\frak{m}_1,...,\tilde\frak{m}_r$. Moreover, $S^\wedge = (S^\wedge)^{\wedge,IS^\wedge}$ and 
$S^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i} = (S^{\wedge, I})^{\wedge,\tilde\frak{m}_i}$
(Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.2. and {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.11.). Then  Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem}}}
gives 
$ S^{\wedge,I} \cong  S^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_1}} \oplus ...\oplus S^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_r}}$, the isomorphisms for 
$S^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}}$ and the direct sum for 
$S^{\wedge,I}/ I S^{\wedge,I}$.

2. By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6. we have  
$M^{\wedge, I}\cong M \otimes_S S^{\wedge,I}$ and
$M\otimes_S S^{\wedge, \frak{m}_i} \cong M^{\wedge, \frak{m}_i}$. The result for $M^{\wedge,I}$ follows then from the result for $S^{\wedge,I}$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop.sem}
Let  $A \to R$ be a morphism of Noetherian rings,  $I\subset R$ an ideal such that $R/I$ is finite over $A$, and $\frak{p}$ a prime ideal of $A$.  
Then:
\medskip

\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $R_\frak{p}/\frak{p}  R_\frak{p}+I R_\frak{p}= \hat R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p}+I \hat R_\frak{p}$, $\hat R_\frak{p} = (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$, is (if non-zero) an Artinian semi-local ring, with maximal ideals $\bar \frak{m}_1,...,\bar \frak{m}_r$ and isomorphic to the direct sum of its localizations at $\bar \frak{m}_i$.\\

\@par\hangindent 2em The ideals  $\frak{n}_i := \bar \frak{m}_i \cap R_\frak{p}$, resp. $\hat \frak{n}_i := \bar \frak{m}_i \cap \hat R_\frak{p}$, $i=1,...,r$, are maximal ideals of $R_\frak{p}$ resp. $\hat R_\frak{p}$.
The ideals $K:=\sqrt{\frak{p} R_\frak{p} + I R_\frak{p}} = \frak{n}_1\cap...\cap \frak{n}_r$ resp. $\hat K := \sqrt{\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p} + I \hat R_\frak{p}} = \hat \frak{n}_1\cap\ldots\cap \hat \frak{n}_r$ satisfy
$$
\begin{array}{llll}
R_\frak{p} / K &=R_\frak{p}/\frak{n}_1 \oplus...\oplus R_\frak{p}/\frak{n}_r,\\
\hat R_\frak{p} / \hat K &= \hat R_\frak{p}/\hat\frak{n}_1 \oplus...\oplus \hat R_\frak{p}/\hat\frak{n}_r.
\end{array}
$$
\medskip
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $\tilde R_\frak{p} := (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, \frak{p}}$ be the $\frak{p} R_\frak{p}$-adic completion of $R_\frak{p}$\footnote{$\tilde R_\frak{p}$  may be considered as a formal neighbourhood of the usual fiber $R_\frak{p}$.}.
The $I$-adic completion $(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$ 
of $\tilde R_\frak{p}$ is (if non-zero) a semi-local ring with maximal ideals 
$\tilde\frak{m}_1,\ldots,\tilde\frak{m}_r$,  $\tilde\frak{m}_i = (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} \cap \bar \frak{m}_i = \frak{n}_i (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$,
and  Jacobson radical
$\operatorname{rad}((\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}) =
\tilde \frak{m}_1\cap\ldots\cap \tilde \frak{m}_r = 
\sqrt{\frak{p} (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}  + I (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}}$. 
                                              
We have a direct sum 
$$(R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, \frak{p} + I}=(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} \cong  (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_1} \oplus ...\oplus (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, \frak{n}_r},$$
with $(R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,{ \frak{n}_i}} \cong ((R_\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,{ \frak{n}_i}}
\cong ((\tilde R_\frak{p})_{\tilde \frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,{\tilde \frak{m}_i}}\cong  (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\tilde \frak{m}_i}} \cong ((\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I})_{\tilde \frak{m}_i}$ being  complete local rings. Moreover $(\tilde R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} \tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}=(R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}= \hat R(\frak{p})$. 
\\
If $M$ is a finite $R$-module, then  
$$ (M_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{p}+I} \cong  (M_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_1} \oplus ...\oplus (M_\frak{p})^{\wedge, \frak{n}_r},$$
with $(M_\frak{p})^{\wedge,{ \frak{n}_i}}\cong M_\frak{p} \otimes_{R_\frak{p}}(R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}} \cong ((M_\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,{ \frak{n}_i}} =(M_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,{ \frak{n}_i}} = M^{\wedge,{ \frak{n}_i}} $.\\

\@par\hangindent 2em The completed fiber $\hat R(\frak{p})=R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$ is (if non-zero) a semi-local ring with maximal ideals $\frak{m}_i = \hat R(\frak{p}) \cap\bar\frak{m}_i = \frak{n}_i \hat R(\frak{p})$, $i=1,...,r$, and  $\operatorname{rad}(\hat R(\frak{p})) =\frak{m}_1\cap\ldots\cap \frak{m}_r = \sqrt{I \hat R(\frak{p})}$.  
The localizations $\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}
\cong  (\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}\cong \hat R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_i}\cong (R(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i} \cong R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i}$ are complete local rings
and $\hat R(\frak{p})$ is a direct sum
$$\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong  R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_r}.$$
The residue field $k(\frak{m}_i):= \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}/\frak{m}_i \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}\cong R_\frak{p}/\frak{n}_i R_\frak{p}$ of $\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}$
is  finite over $k(\frak{p})$.\\
If $M$ is a finite $R$-module, then  
$$\hat M(\frak{p})\cong \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_r}$$
and $\hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i} \cong  (\hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}} \cong \hat M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}} \cong M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}} = (M(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}} $.  
Moreover
$ \hat M(\frak{p})= (M_\frak{p}/\frak{p} M_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}= (M_\frak{p}/\frak{p} M_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{p}+I}$. 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
The following commutative diagram makes the relation between the different rings and maximal ideals clear. 
\[
\begin{xymatrix}{
R_\frak{p} \ar[r]^{\frak{n}_i \quad\quad \hat \frak{n}_i} \ar@{->>}[d]& \hat R_\frak{p} \ar[r]^{ \quad\quad \tilde \frak{m}_i} \ar@{->>}[d]_{\hat\frak{n}_i \mapsto \frak{m}_i  }
& (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, I} \ar@{->>}[d]^{\tilde \frak{m}_i \mapsto \bar \frak{m}_i} \ar@{->>}[dl] \\
R(\frak{p}) \ar[r] & \hat R(\frak{p}) \ar@{->>}[r] &  {\hat R(\frak{p})/I \hat R(\frak{p})} & \mkern -46mu = \hat R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} \hat R_\frak{p}+I \hat R_\frak{p}
}
\end{xymatrix}
\relax
    \ifmmode
      \egroup \m@th$\hfil \egroup
      \end{trivlist} 
    \else \@badmath
    \fi
The maximal ideals $\bar \frak{m}_i \subset \hat R(\frak{p})/I \hat R(\frak{p})
= R(\frak{p})/I R(\frak{p})$ are the starting point, the other maximal ideals $\frak{n}_i \subset  R_\frak{p}$, $\hat \frak{n}_i \subset  \hat R_\frak{p}$, $\tilde \frak{m}_i \subset  (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$, and $\frak{m}_i \subset  \hat R(\frak{p})$ are the preimages of  $\bar \frak{m}_i$ (that these are maximal ideals will be shown below). The last upper horizontal arrow is $\hat R_\frak{p} = (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, I} \to (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, I+\frak{p}} = (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$ and the
diagonal arrow is the composition 
$(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge, I} \twoheadrightarrow (\tilde R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} \tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}=
((R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{p}} /\frak{p} (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{p}} )^{\wedge,I}
=
(R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}= \hat R(\frak{p})$ 
(using Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.3.). The other arrows are the canonical ones.
\\

1.  Since $S:=R/I$ is finite over $A$, the fiber $S(\frak{p})= R_\frak{p}/\frak{p}  R_\frak{p}+I R_\frak{p}
=\hat R_\frak{p}/\frak{p}  \hat R_\frak{p}+I \hat R_\frak{p}$ is a semi-local direct sum with maximal ideals $\bar\frak{m}_1,...,\bar\frak{m}_r$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.1..

2. $S_\frak{p} = R_\frak{p}/I R_\frak{p}=\hat R_\frak{p}/I \hat R_\frak{p}$ is a semi-local direct sum with maximal ideals $S_\frak{p} \cap \bar \frak{m}_i$, $i=1,...,r$. As a preimage from $S_\frak{p}$, $\frak{n}_i = R_\frak{p} \cap \bar \frak{m}_i$ contains $\frak{p} R_\frak{p}+IR_\frak{p}$ and hence $R_\frak{p} /\frak{n}_i= S(\frak{p})/ \bar \frak{m}_i$ is a field, showing that $\frak{n}_i$ is a maximal ideal. The same holds for the ideals $\hat\frak{n}_i$.
$R_\frak{p}/\frak{p}  R_\frak{p}+I R_\frak{p}$ is an Artinian semi-local ring and so is its reduction $R_\frak{p}/K$, which is therefore the direct sum of the fields $R_\frak{p}/\frak{n}_i$. 
Hence $K=\sqrt{\frak{p}  R_\frak{p}+I R_\frak{p}}=\frak{n}_1\cap...\cap\frak{n}_r$.

3. By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.11.  and  {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.1. we have $(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} = (R_\frak{p}) ^{\wedge, \frak{p} + I} = (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,K}$. $K=\sqrt{\frak{p} R_\frak{p} + I R_\frak{p}}$. 
We deduce from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem2\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem2}}} that 
$((R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,K})_{\tilde\frak{m}_i}\cong (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}} 
\cong ((R_\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}}$ 
is semi-local with maximal ideals 
$\frak{n}_i(R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,K}=\frak{n}_i(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$. Since $\frak{n}_i = \tilde\frak{m}_i \cap R_\frak{p}$, the maximal ideal $\frak{n}_i(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$ is contained 
in $\tilde\frak{m}_i$ and hence  
$\tilde \frak{m}_i =\frak{n}_i(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$.
Moreover,  Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem2\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem2}}} gives
 $(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\tilde\frak{m}_i} = (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i}
= ((R_\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}}$, 
$$(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} =\oplus_{i=1}^r (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\tilde\frak{m}_i}=\oplus_{i=1}^r (R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i},$$
and $\tilde \frak{m}_1\cap\ldots\cap \tilde \frak{m}_r = K (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$= 
$\sqrt{\frak{p} (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} + I (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}}$. 

4. Since $\tilde R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} \tilde R_\frak{p}= R_\frak{p}/ \frak{p} R_\frak{p} = R(\frak{p})$ (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.3.) and since $(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$
is semi-local with maximal ideals $\tilde\frak{m}_i$
by 3., we get that $(\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}/\frak{p} (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}=
R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}= \hat R(\frak{p})$ is semi-local 
with maximal ideals $\tilde \frak{m}_i \hat R(\frak{p})$
$i=1,...,r$. Since $\frak{m}_i = \hat R(\frak{p}) \cap \bar \frak{m}_i 
= \hat R(\frak{p}) \cap \tilde \frak{m}_i $ (by the diagram above) we get $\tilde \frak{m}_i \hat R(\frak{p})=\frak{m}_i$.
The remaining statements follow from step 3.
For the residue field $k(\frak{m}_i)$ we get $\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}/\frak{m}_i \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}\cong R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i}/\frak{n}_i R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i} \cong (R(\frak{p})/\frak{n}_i R(\frak{p}))^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i} \cong (R_\frak{p}/\frak{n}_i R_\frak{p}))^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i} \cong 
 R_\frak{p}/\frak{n}_i R_\frak{p}.$
 It is finite over $k(\frak{p})$ since $\hat R(\frak{p})/I\hat R(\frak{p})=R(\frak{p})/IR(\frak{p})$ is finite over $k(\frak{p})$ and $I\hat R(\frak{p}) \subset \frak{m}_i$.

Since $\hat M(\frak{p})= M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,I} = M(\frak{p})\otimes_{R(\frak{p})}R(\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}= M(\frak{p})\otimes_{R(\frak{p})} \hat R(\frak{p})$
(Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6. and Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.cpl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.cpl}}}) and 
$\hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}=
\hat M(\frak{p})\otimes_{\hat R(\frak{p})}\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i} $
 (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.11.15, tag 00DK]{Sta19})
we get
$$\hat M(\frak{p}) \cong \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_r}$$
and (by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem2\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem2}}} and since $I\hat R(\frak{p}) \subset \frak{m}_i$) $\hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i} \cong  (\hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i})^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}} \cong \hat M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\frak{m}_i}} \cong M(\frak{p})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_i}} $.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{rm.sem0}{\em
(1) It is shown in Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.open\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.open}}} that $\hat M(\frak{p}) \ne 0$ iff $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(M/I)$ and that 
$$\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM) = \operatorname{Supp}_R(M)\cap V(I) \to 
\operatorname{Supp}_A(M/I) =\{\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \mid \hat M(\frak{p}) \ne 0 \},$$
$\frak{n} \mapsto \frak{p} = \frak{n}\cap A$, is surjective and finite. \\
It follows from Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}} for $M=R$ that the prime ideals in $V(I)\subset \operatorname{Spec} R$ lying over $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(R/I)$ are exactly the $\tilde \frak{n}_i =R\cap \bar\frak{m}_i =R\cap \frak{m}_i$, $i=1,...,r_\frak{p}$, with $\bar\frak{m}_i$ resp. $\frak{m}_i$ the maximal ideals of $\hat R(\frak{p})/I$ resp. of  $\hat R(\frak{p})$ from Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.1. 
It follows for $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$, using 
$\hat M(\frak{p})=M(\frak{p})\otimes_{R(\frak{p})}\hat R(\frak{p})$,
that 
$$\hat M(\frak{p})_\frak{n} \ne 0 \iff \frak{n} \in \operatorname{Supp}_R(M)\cap V(I),
\ \frak{p} = \frak{n} \cap A.$$
The maximal ideals $\frak{n}_i =R_\frak{p} \cap \bar \frak{m}_i$, $i=1,...,{r_\frak{p}}$, of $R_\frak{p}$ are exactly the 
$\tilde \frak{n}_i R_\frak{p}$. The ideal $\tilde \frak{n}_i$ is a prime ideal in $R$ containing $\frak{p} + I$. It is a maximal ideal if $\frak{p}$ is a maximal ideal of $A$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}, since $R/\tilde \frak{n}_i = R/I+\tilde \frak{n}_i$ and $R/I$ is finite over $A$ (see also the footnote there).

(2) While  $\hat R_\frak{p}$ and $\hat R(\frak{p})$  are semi-local, this may not  be the case with $R_\frak{p}$ and $R(\frak{p})$ for non-maximal $\frak{p}$. See Example {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@ex.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{ex.sem0}}} (1), where $R_\frak{p}=R(\frak{p})$ has infinitely many maximal ideals.
}
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{ex.sem0}
{\em The following examples illustrate Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}} and show the difference between usual fiber and the completed fiber for a non-maximal prime $\frak{p} \subset A$. 
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em [(1)] In contrast to the completed fiber $\hat R(\frak{p})$, the usual fibre $R(\frak{p})$ may not be semi-local:
Consider $A=K[t]$,  $K$ a field, $I=\langle x \rangle \subset A[x]$, $R=A[[x]]$ with $t$ and $x$ one variable. For
$\frak{p} =\langle 0 \rangle \in  \operatorname{Spec} A$, we have $A_\frak{p} = k(\frak{p}) = K(t)$, and
$$R(\frak{p}) = R_\frak{p}  = K[t][[x]] \otimes_{K[t]} K(t) =  \{g / h \, | \, g \in K[t][[x]],  h \in K[t]\smallsetminus 0\},$$
a subring strictly contained in  $\hat R(\frak{p})=\hat R_\frak{p} = R\hat\otimes_A A_\frak{p}  = K(t)[[x]]$ (see also \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Example 2.2]{GP21}). For $c\in K$,
$K[t][[x]]/\langle t-c-x\rangle\cong K[[t]]$ via $f(t,x)\mapsto f(t+c,t)$ and hence $R_{\frak{p}}/\langle t-c-x\rangle= (K[t][[x]]/\langle t-c-x\rangle)_\frak{p} 
\cong K[[t]]_{\langle 0 \rangle}  \cong K((t))$
is a field. Hence $R(\frak{p}) = R_\frak{p}$ has infinitely many maximal 
 ideals $\langle t-c-x\rangle$ if $K$ is infinite. On the other hand, $\hat R(\frak{p})$ is a local ring.


\@par\hangindent 2em [(2)] A module $M$ with $\hat M_\frak{p} =\hat M(\frak{p}) =0$ while $\dim_{k(\frak{p})} M(\frak{p}) = \infty$ (see \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Example 2.4]{GP21} for more details):\\
Let $A=\mathbbm{Z}$, $I=\langle x \rangle \subset \mathbbm{Z}[x]$, $R = \mathbbm{Z}[x]^{\wedge,I}=\mathbbm{Z}[[x]]$, and $M = R/\langle x-p\rangle$, $p \in \mathbbm{Z}$ a prime number. Since $R= \lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\mathbbm{Z}[x]/\langle x \rangle^n $ we obtain
$M = \lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\mathbbm{Z}/p^n = \hat \mathbbm{Z}_{\langle p\rangle}$, the ring of 
$p$-adic integers.
For the prime ideal $\langle 0\rangle$ we get 
$\hat M(\langle 0\rangle)= M\hat \otimes_\mathbbm{Z} \mathbbm{Q} =\mathbbm{Q}[[x]]/\langle x-p\rangle=0,$
while
$M(\langle 0\rangle)= M\otimes_\mathbbm{Z} \mathbbm{Q} = \hat \mathbbm{Z}_{\langle p\rangle}\otimes_\mathbbm{Z} \mathbbm{Q} =\operatorname{Quot}(\hat \mathbbm{Z}_{\langle p\rangle}) $ 
is infinite dimensional over $\mathbbm{Q}=k(\langle 0\rangle)$. 

\@par\hangindent 2em [(3)] Let  $A=K[t]$,  $K$ an algebraically closed field, $I=\langle x \rangle \subset A[x]$, $R=A[x]^{\wedge,\langle x \rangle}=A[[x]]$ with $t$ and $x$ one variable, and 
$M= R/\langle t-x \rangle \cong K[[t]] $ as $A$-module. We have $\operatorname{Supp}_A (M) = \operatorname{Spec} A$ and
$$ \{\frak{p} \,|\,\hat d_\frak{p}(M)\neq0\} = \{\langle t \rangle\} \subsetneqq
\{\frak{p} \,|\,d_\frak{p}(M)\neq0\}= \{\langle t \rangle , \langle 0 \rangle\}  \subsetneqq \operatorname{Supp}_A (M) $$ (see \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Example 2.3 (4)]{GP21}).
\end{enumerate}
 }
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop.sem2}
In addition to  the assumptions of Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}
 assume moreover that 
$\frak{p}\in \operatorname{Spec} A$  is a {\em maximal} ideal.  Let  $\frak{m}_1,...,\frak{m}_r$  be the maximal ideals of the semi-local ring 
$\hat R(\frak{p}) = \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_r}$
(Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}). Denote by $\varphi:A\to R$ the given map.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em 
For each $1\le i \le r $ the preimage $\tilde\frak{n}_i$ in $R$ of $\frak{m}_i$ is a maximal ideal, and there exists an  open neigbourhood  $\operatorname{Spec} A_i \subset \operatorname{Spec} A$ of $\frak{p}$, 
$A_i = A_{g_i}$ for some $g_i \in A$,
and 
 an open neigbourhood $U_i=\operatorname{Spec} Q_i \subset \operatorname{Spec} R$ of $\tilde\frak{n}_i$,  $Q_i = R_{\varphi(g_i)}$,
 such that  
 $\tilde\frak{n}_j \notin U_i$ if $j\ne i$, 
and  $Q_i/IQ_i$ is finite over $A_i$. \\
Moreover, for $\frak{q} \in \operatorname{Spec} A_i$ such that $\hat R(\frak{q}) \ne 0$, the ring $\hat Q_i(\frak{q})$ is semi-local with maximal ideals being a subset of the maximal ideals of the semi-local ring $\hat R(\frak{q})$.
                                                                                                           
                                                                                                                          
                                                                              
                                            
                                                                                                                                                                                                       
\@par\hangindent 2em Assume furthermore that $R$ is  of finite type and flat over $A$.
Then $Q_i$ and $A_i$ can be chosen such that 
$\operatorname{Spec} Q_i \to \operatorname{Spec} A_i$ is flat and open with non-empty fibers $\operatorname{Spec} Q_i(\frak{q})$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
1. Consider the following sequence,
$$R\longrightarrow R_\frak{p}\longrightarrow (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}
\longrightarrow  \hat R(\frak{p}) = (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}/\frak{p} (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}.$$
Denote by $\tilde\frak{n}_i$ the preimage in $R$ of $\frak{m}_i = \tilde \frak{m}_i \hat R(\frak{p})$, which is also the preimage of $\tilde \frak{m}_i \subset (\tilde R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}$. 
Then  $\tilde\frak{n}_i$ is a maximal ideal  by Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sem0}}} (1), since $\frak{p}$ is maximal.

Now choose $f_i\in \tilde\frak{n}_1\cap\tilde\frak{n}_2\ldots\cap \tilde\frak{n}_{i-1}\cap\tilde\frak{n}_{i+1}\ldots\tilde\frak{n}_r$ such that $f_i\notin \tilde\frak{n}_i$  and $\tilde\frak{n}_i \in D(f_j)$ iff $i=j$. Let $\varphi$ denote the given map $A\to R$.
$\operatorname{Spec} (\varphi)(D(f_i))$ can be covered by suitable affine open sets $D(g_{ik})\subset \operatorname{Spec} A$ and with
 $Q_{ik}:=R_{\varphi(g_{ik})}$ we get that $Q_{ik}/IQ_{ik} = (R/IR)_{\varphi(g_{ik})}$ is finite over $A_{g_{ik}}$. For a given $i$ there exists $k$ such that $\tilde\frak{n}_i\in \operatorname{Spec} Q_{ik}$ and $\tilde\frak{n}_j\notin \operatorname{Spec} Q_{ik}$ for $i\neq j$. With $Q_i:=Q_{ik}$ and $A_i:=A_{g_{ik}}$ 
 we can apply Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}} to $A_i \to Q_i$, which proves the first part of 
 the proposition.
 
2. Since $R$ is flat over $A$ and of finite type the corresponding morphism of affine schemes is open 
(\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[00I1.]{Sta19}) and thus $Q_i$ and $A_i$ can be chosen such that 
$\operatorname{Spec} Q_i \to \operatorname{Spec} A_i$ is open with non-empty fibers.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
\label{ex.max}{\em 
For $\frak{q} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ which is not a maximal ideal, the completed fiber $\operatorname{Spec} \hat R(\frak{q})$ may be empty while the fiber  $\operatorname{Spec} R(\frak{q})$ is non-empty (under the assumption of Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem2\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem2}}}.2.):

Let $A=K[t]$, $P= A[x]/\langle t-x \rangle =K[t] =A$, $I=\langle t \rangle$. Then
$R=P^{\wedge, I} =K[[t]]$ and with $\frak{q} = \langle 0\rangle$
we have $R_\frak{q}=R(\frak{q})=K((t))$ but, since $t$ is a unit in $K((t))$,
$\hat R_\frak{q}=\hat R(\frak{q})=K((t))^{\wedge, \langle t \rangle} =\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}K((t))/t^n=0$,  i.e. $\operatorname{Spec} \hat R(\frak{q}) = \emptyset$.}
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->
\label{ex.sem}
{\em
Consider the ideal  $I=\langle x^2+1\rangle\subset \mathbb Z[x]$ and let $R=\mathbb Z[x]^{\wedge,I}$. Let $p\in\mathbb Z$ be a prime, $\frak{p}=\langle p \rangle \subset \mathbb Z[x]$ the corresponding prime ideal.
We determine $\hat R(\frak{p})$ for different choices of $\frak{p}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em If $p\not\equiv 1 \text{ mod } 4$ then\footnote{The polynomial $x^2+1$ splits in $ (\mathbbm{Z}/p)[x]$ iff $p\equiv 1 \text{ mod } 4$.}
$\hat R(\frak{p})=(\mathbbm{Z}/p)[x]^{\wedge,I}$ is a complete discrete valuation ring. $I$ is a maximal ideal in $(\mathbbm{Z}/p)[x]$ and 
$(\mathbbm{Z}/p)[x]^{\wedge,I} \cong (\mathbbm{Z}/p)(i)[[x]]$.
\@par\hangindent 2em If $p\equiv 1 \text{ mod } 4$ then                                                                                                 choose $i\in \mathbb Z/p$ with $i^2+1=0$.
$\hat R(\frak{p})=\mathbb (Z/p)[x]^{\wedge,I}=\mathbb (Z/p)[x]^{\wedge,{\langle x+i\rangle}} \oplus \mathbb (Z/p)[x]^{\wedge,{\langle x-i\rangle}}$ is a semi-local ring, isomorphic to $(\mathbbm{Z}/p)[[x]] \oplus (\mathbbm{Z}/p)[[x]]$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $\hat R({\langle 0\rangle})=\mathbb Q[x]^{\wedge,I}$ is a complete discrete valuation ring, which is isomorphic to $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-1})[[x]]$.
\end{enumerate}}
<!-- end example-4 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem.free}
Let $A$ be a ring and $M$ a finitely presented  $A$-module. Then $d_\frak{p}(M) = \dim_{k(\frak{p})} M(\frak{p})$
 is semicontinuous on  $\operatorname{Spec} A$. If, moreover, $M$ is flat over $A$, then $M$ is locally free and $d_\frak{p}(M)$ is locally constant on  $\operatorname{Spec} A$. Conversely, if $A$ is reduced and $d_\frak{p}(M)$ locally constant on  $\operatorname{Spec} A$, then $M$ is locally free.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Consider a presentation of $M$,
$$ A^p \xrightarrow{\text {$T$}} A^q \to  M \to 0,$$
with matrix $T = (t_{ij}), \ t_{ij}  \in A$. 
For $\frak{p}\in \operatorname{Spec} A$ 
let $T(\frak{p})$ be the matrix with entries the images of $t_{ij}$ in $k(\frak{p})$. 
Then  $\dim_{k(\frak{p})} M\otimes_A k(\frak{p}) = q - \operatorname{rank}(T(\frak{p}))$, which is upper semicontinuous since the rank is lower semicontinuous: If $r = \operatorname{rank}(T(\frak{p}))$, then $\frak{p}$ is contained in the open set $D(f)$, with $f \in A[t_{i,j}]$ an $r$-minor of $T$ and $f(\frak{p}) \ne 0$ in $k(\frak{p})$ (and bigger minors vanish at $\frak{p}$) . Hence, for $\frak{q} \in D(f)$ we have $f(\frak{q}) \ne 0$ in $k(\frak{q})$ and $\operatorname{rank}(T(\frak{q}))\ge r$. 
If $M$ flat, it is locally free and the rank is locally constant (see \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 4.10 and its proof]{Mat86}).

To see the converse, we may assume that $T\neq0$ and that 
$d_\frak{p}(M)$ is constant on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
Let $I$ be the ideal generated by the non-zero $s$-minors $f_1,...,f_k$ of $T$
with $ s= \text{ max } \{l \,| \, \exists \ l\text{-minor} \neq 0 \text{ of } T\}.$
If $I\subset \frak{p}$ for some $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$, then $f_i \in \frak{p} A_\frak{p}$ and
$$d_\frak{p}(M) > q-s.$$
Since $I\neq 0$ and $A$ is reduced, $V(I)\neq \operatorname{Spec} A$ and there is a $\frak{q} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ with $I\not\subset \frak{q}$. This implies 
$$d_\frak{q}(M) = q-s,$$
and since this dimension is constant, $V(I) =\emptyset$. Hence $I=A$ and 
the open sets $D(f_i)$ cover $\operatorname{Spec} A$. 

We claim that 
 $M_{f_i}$ is a free $A_{f_i}$-module on $D(f_i)$. 
 W.l.o.g. let $f_1$ be  the determinant of the upper left $s\times s$-submatrix of $T$. By a change of bases  in $A^p$ and $A^q$ over $A_{f_1}$ we can achieve that $T$ becomes
 $$T_{f_1}=\left[
\begin{array}{c|c}
1_s  & 0 \\ \hline
0 & T'
\end{array}
\right]$$
with $1_s$ the $s\times s$ unit matrix and $T'=0$ because of the maximality of $s$. Then 
$$M_{f_1} \cong A_{f_1}^q /Im(T_{f_1}) =A^{q-s}_{f_1},$$
and similar for all $f_i$. This shows that $M$ is locally free.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- example-5: body_md -->
\label{ex.notsemcont}
{\em
In many interesting applications, however, $M$ is not finitely presented over $A$. A typical case arises when $A$ is a Noetherian ring,  $R=A[[x]]=A[x]^{\wedge,\langle x \rangle} $, and $M$ is a finitely presented $R$-module that is not finite over $A$ (e.g. $R$ itself).

Then the semicontinuity of $d_\frak{p}(M)$ may not hold on $\operatorname{Spec} A$, as the following example shows (see also Example {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@ex.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{ex.sem0}}} (3)).
Let $K$ be an algebraically closed field,  $ A = K[t]$, $R=A[[x]]$,  and $M  = A[[x]]/\langle x-t\rangle \cong K[[t]]$. Then the set of closed points is $\operatorname{Max} A = \{\langle t-c\rangle, c \in K\}$ and $\operatorname{Spec} A = \operatorname{Max} A \cup \{\langle 0 \rangle\}$.
We have $M(\frak{p}) = K$ for $\frak{p}= \langle t\rangle$ and $0$ for 
$\frak{p}= \langle t-c\rangle, \ c \neq 0$, while $M(\langle 0\rangle) \cong K((t))$ and  $k(\langle 0\rangle) =K(t)$. Hence
$\dim_{k(\langle 0\rangle)}M(\langle 0\rangle) = \infty > \dim_{k(\frak{p})}M(\frak{p}) $
for $\frak{p} \neq \langle 0\rangle$ and $d_\frak{p}(M)$ is not semicontinuous at every $\frak{p} \ne \langle 0\rangle$  (the generic point $\langle 0\rangle$ is in the neighbourhood of any other point $\frak{p}$). Since $\dim_{k(\langle 0\rangle)}\hat M(\langle 0\rangle) = 0$ the completed fiber dimension  $\hat d_\frak{p}(M) = \dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})$ is semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
}
<!-- end example-5 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop.Afin}
Let $A \to R$ a map of Noetherian rings and $M$ a finitely generated $R$-module. If  $I\subset R$ is an ideal such that $R/I$ is finite over $A$  the following holds.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em If \, $\operatorname{Supp}_R (M) \subset V(I)$ then  $M$ is finitely generated  over $A$ and
$\hat M(\frak{q}) = M(\frak{q})$ for all $\frak{q} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. In particular,
$\hat d_\frak{p}(M)= d_\frak{p}(M)$ is semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
 \@par\hangindent 2em
If $M$ is finitely generated as an $A$-module,
 then  $\hat d_\frak{p}(M)\leq d_\frak{p}(M)<\infty$ and  $\hat d_\frak{p}(M)$ (as well as $d_\frak{p}(M)$) is semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- example-6: body_md -->
\label{ex.complete} {\em
Let $A=K[[t]]$, $K$ a field, $R=A[[x]]$, and let
$M = R/\langle t-x\rangle \cong K[[t]]$.  For the  two prime ideals $\langle 0\rangle$
and $\langle t\rangle$ of $A$ we get:
$k(\langle 0\rangle) = K((t))$,  $k(\langle t\rangle) = K$,
$M(\langle 0\rangle) \cong K[[t]]\otimes_{K[[t]]}K((t)) = K((t))$, $M(\langle t\rangle) \cong K$
and $d_{\langle 0\rangle}(M) =d_{\langle t\rangle}(M) =1$. Hence  $d_{\frak{p}}(M)$
is semicontinuous (even continuous) on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.

$\hat M(\langle 0\rangle) \cong K((t))[[x]]/\langle t-x\rangle =0$, $\hat M(\langle t\rangle) \cong K$
and $\hat d_{\langle 0\rangle}(M) =0$, $\hat d_{\langle t\rangle}(M) =1$. 
Hence  $\hat d_{\frak{p}}(M)$
is semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$,
$M$ is finitely generated as $A$-module, and we have 
$\hat d_{\langle 0\rangle}(M) < d_{\langle 0\rangle}(M)$.
}
<!-- end example-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem.ann}  
Let $A\to R$ be a morphism of rings and $M$ an $R$-module.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em Considering $M$ as $A$-module, we have 
  $\operatorname{Ann}_A(M) = \operatorname{Ann}_R(M) \cap A$. If 
  $M$  is  finite over $R$ then  
  $$\operatorname{Supp}_A(M) = V(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M))$$
and  hence $\operatorname{Supp}_A(M)$ is closed in $\operatorname{Spec} A$ and the natural map $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M) \to \operatorname{Supp}_A(M)$ is dominant. 
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $R$ be Noetherian, $I$ an ideal in $R$, $M$   finite over $R$, and $M^\wedge$ the $I$-adic completion of $M$. 
Then 
$$M^\wedge=0 \iff IM = M \iff I^n M = M \text{ for all } n\ge 1,$$
and this is equivalent to $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M)\cap V(I)= \emptyset$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
1. Let $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. Note that $\frak{p} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_AM \Leftrightarrow M_\frak{p}=0 \Leftrightarrow \forall m\in M \  \exists f \in A$, $f \! \notin \! \frak{p}$,  $fm=0$ and that 
$\frak{p} \notin V(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M)) \Leftrightarrow  \operatorname{Ann}_A(M) \nsubseteq \frak{p}   \Leftrightarrow   \exists f \! \notin \! \frak{p}, f M= 0 $. Hence $\frak{p} \notin V(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M))$ implies $\frak{p} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_A(M)$, i.e. $\operatorname{Supp}_A (M) \subset V(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M))$. 
Now let $M$ be generated over $R$ by $m_1,\dots,m_k \in M$. If $M_\frak{p}=0$, choose  $f_i \in A, f_i \! \notin \! \frak{p},  f_im_i=0.$ 
Then 
$f=f_1\cdots f_k \! \notin \! \frak{p}$ satisfies $fM=0 $ and hence $f\in \operatorname{Ann}_A(M)$. We get $\frak{p} \notin V(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M))$ and hence the other inclusion $\operatorname{Supp}_A (M) \supset V(\operatorname{Ann}_A(M))$.
Then  $A/\operatorname{Ann}_A(M) \to R/\operatorname{Ann}_R(M)$ is injective and hence $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M) \to \operatorname{Supp}_A(M)$ is dominant (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 28.8.6, tag 0CC1]{Sta19}).

2. $ IM = M$ is, by induction on $n$,  equivalent to  $I^n M = M$ for all $n\ge 1$.
Since $\cap_{n=1}^\infty I^nM$ is the kernel of $M \to M^\wedge$ (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.9.),
$M^\wedge =0$ implies $M=I^n M$ for all $n$.\\
Conversely, let  $M=I M$. Then, since 
$M  / I M=M ^\wedge / I M^\wedge$ by Lemma  {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.3. we get
$M ^\wedge / I M^\wedge =0$. Since  $M ^\wedge$ is an $R^\wedge$-module and $R^\wedge$ is $I$-adically complete, $I\subset \operatorname{rad} (R^\wedge)$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.4. Hence
$M^\wedge=IM^\wedge$ implies $M^\wedge=0$ by  Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.10. (Nakayama).\\
The last statement follows since $M^\wedge =0  \iff M/IM=0
\iff \operatorname{Supp}_R (M/IM) = \operatorname{Supp}_R(M)\cap V(I) = \emptyset$.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem.ann1}
Let $R$ be Noetherian, $I\subset R$ an ideal, $M$ a finitely generated $R$-module and $M^\wedge$ the $I$-adic completion of $M$. For $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$ the following holds.
$$(M_\frak{n})^{\wedge} = 0 \iff \frak{n} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_R(M)\cap V(I)
=\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM).$$
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ann\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ann}}}.2. we have
$(M_\frak{n})^\wedge=(M_\frak{n})^{\wedge, IR_\frak{n}}=0 \iff IM_\frak{n} = M_\frak{n}$.
Since $M_\frak{n}$ is finitely generated over the local  ring $R_\frak{n}$, we get from Nakayama's Lemma
$$IM_\frak{n} = M_\frak{n} \iff M_\frak{n} = 0 \text{ or } IR_\frak{n} =R_\frak{n}
\iff \frak{n} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_R(M) \text{ or }  \frak{n} \notin V(I)$$
which is equivalent to  $\frak{n} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_R(M) \cap V(I)$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem.open} Let $A\to R$ be a morphism of Noetherian rings, $M$ a finitely generated $R$-module and $I\subset R$ an ideal such that $R/I$ is finite over $A$.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em We have (possibly strict) inclusions 
$$\{ \frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \ | \ \hat d_\frak{p}(M) \neq 0\} \subset \{\frak{p} \,|\,d_\frak{p}(M)\neq0\}  \subset \operatorname{Supp}_A (M),$$
where the first and the last  sets are closed in $\operatorname{Spec} A$, while the middle set may not be closed.
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. Then  
$$\hat M(\frak{p})=M(\frak{p})^{\wedge} = 0 \iff \frak{p} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_A(M/IM).$$
 Hence $\{ \frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \ | \ \hat d_\frak{p} (M) =0\}$, the {\em vanishing locus} of $\hat d_\frak{p} (M)$, is open in $\operatorname{Spec} A$. 
Moreover, the natural map
$$
  \operatorname{Supp}_{R}(M/IM)= V(I) \cap \operatorname{Supp}_{R}(M)
 \longrightarrow  
  \bigl\{\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \mid \hat{d}_{\frak{p}}(M) \neq 0\bigr\},
$$
$  \frak{q} \mapsto \frak{q} \cap A$,
is finite and surjective.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
1. Both inclusions follows from the definitions. For an example where these inclusions are strict, see
Example {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@ex.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{ex.sem0}}} (2) and (3). In Example {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@ex.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{ex.sem0}}} (3) the middle set is not closed. The closedness of the first set follows from 2.


2. From Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ann\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ann}}}.2 we deduce
 $M(\frak{p})^{\wedge} = 0  \iff M(\frak{p})/I M(\frak{p})= 0$.
Since $M(\frak{p})/I M(\frak{p}) 
  \cong (M/IM) \otimes_A k(\frak{p})$ and since $M/IM$ is finite over $A$, $(M/IM)_\frak{p}$ is finite over $A_\frak{p}$.
  We get from Nakayama's lemma that 
 $ (M/IM) \otimes_A k(\frak{p}) =0  \iff (M/IM)_\frak{p}=0 \iff 
  \frak{p} \notin \operatorname{Supp}_A(M/IM)$.
Since
$\operatorname{Supp}_A (M/IM)$
 is closed in $\operatorname{Spec} A$, the  vanishing locus of $\hat d_\frak{p} (M)$ is open in $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
 
  Moreover, we get from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ann\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ann}}}.1 that the  map 
  $$ V(I) \cap \operatorname{Supp}_{R}(M)=\operatorname{Supp}_{R} (M/IM) \to \operatorname{Supp}_A (M/IM)$$
   is dominant. It is finite since $\operatorname{Spec} (R/I)\to \operatorname{Spec} (A)$ is finite. It is surjective, since finite maps are closed (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 01WM]{Sta19}).
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm.scont}
Let  $A \to R$ be a morphism of Noetherian rings,  $I\subset R$ an ideal such that $R/I$ is finite over $A$, and $M$ a finitely generated $R$-module.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em  Then $\hat d_\frak{p}(M) = \dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})$ is semicontinuous on 
$\operatorname{Spec} A$. 
\@par\hangindent 2em If $M$ is flat over $A$, then $\hat d_\frak{p}(M)$ is locally constant on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Since $M$ is finite over $R$, $I^nM$ is finite over $R$ and $I^nM/I^{n+1}M$ is finite over $R/I$ for each $n\ge 0$.
Since $R/I$ is finite over $A$, $I^nM/I^{n+1}M$ is finite over $A$ for each $n\ge 0$. From the exact sequence
  $$0 \to I^nM / I^{n+1} M\to M/ I^{n+1} M\to M/ I^nM \to 0$$
it follows by induction that $M/ I^n M$ is finite over $A$ for all $n\ge1$. 
In the same way $I^n/ I^{n+1}$ and $R/ I^n$ are finite over $A$ for all $n\ge1$. 


Fix $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. 
We have (by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.6.)
that $ \hat M (\frak{p}) = M (\frak{p})^{\wedge}$  is a finite 
$ \hat R (\frak{p})$-module.
To prove semicontinuity of $\dim_{k(\frak{p})}\hat M(\frak{p})$ at $\frak{p}$, we may assume  that $\hat M (\frak{p})\ne 0$ (by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.open\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.open}}}.2) and that $\dim_{k(\frak{p})}\hat M(\frak{p})< \infty$ (otherwise semicontinuity at $\frak{p}$ is trivial).
Then  $I\hat M (\frak{p})$ is strictly contained in $\hat M (\frak{p})$ by Nakayama's lemma (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.10.),
since $I\hat R(\frak{p})$ is contained in the Jacobson radical of  $\hat R (\frak{p})$
by Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.3.
This implies 
$I^N \hat M(\frak{p}) =
 (I^N M) \hat \oplus_A k(\frak{p}) = 0 $ for some  $N =N(\frak{p}) \le \dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})$ and hence 
$(I^{n} M) \hat \oplus_A k(\frak{p}) =0$ for $n\ge N$. Again by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.open\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.open}}}.2 it follows that $(I^{n} M) \hat \oplus_A k(\frak{q}) =I^n \hat M(\frak{q})=0$ for $n\ge N$ and for $\frak{q}$ in some open neighbourhood $U$ of $\frak{p}$.

Since $M/I^nM = \hat M/I^n\hat M$ is finite over $A$ for $n\ge 1$, 
$$\dim_{k(\frak{p})}(\hat M/I^{n}\hat M) \otimes_{A} k(\frak{p}) =
\dim_{k(\frak{p})}\hat M(\frak{p})/I^{n}\hat M(\frak{p})$$ is semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.free\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.free}}}. We use now that $I^{n}\hat M(\frak{q}) =0$ for $n\ge N$ and $\frak{q} \in U$ to deduce  
 that  $\dim_{k(\frak{q})}\hat M(\frak{q}) = \dim_{k(\frak{q})}\hat M(\frak{q})/I^{n}\hat M(\frak{q})$ is semicontinuous on $U$. 
 Since $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ was arbitrary, the semicontinuity of $\dim_{k(\frak{q})}\hat M(\frak{q})$ on $\operatorname{Spec} A$ follows. 
 
  If $M$ is flat over $A$, then $\dim_{k(\frak{q})}\hat M(\frak{q})$ is locally constant on  $\operatorname{Spec} A$, again by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.free\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.free}}}.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rm.cont}
{\em
(1) For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ with $\hat R(\frak{p}) \ne 0$, the completed fiber $\hat R(\frak{p})$ is a
semi-local ring
with maximal ideals $\frak{m}_1,...\frak{m}_r$, isomorphic to   
$\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_r}$  (Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}).
 $\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}$ is a complete local ring with residue field
$k(\frak{m}_i) = \hat R(\frak{p})/\frak{m}_i$, which is finite over $k(\frak{p})$.
Moreover, 
$\hat M(\frak{p})\cong \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_1}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_r}$ and hence 
$$\dim_{k(\frak{p})}  \hat M(\frak{p})=
\sum_{i=1}^r \dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}=
\sum_{i=1}^r [k(\frak{m}_i):k(\frak{p})]\dim_{k(\frak{m}_i)} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}.$$ 

(2) Assume that $\frak{p}$ is a {\em maximal}  ideal of $A$. Let $\frak{m}_i$ be one of the maximal ideals of 
$\hat R(\frak{p})$ and $\tilde \frak{n}_i =\frak{m}_i \cap R$ the preimage of $\frak{m}_i$ in $R$. Then, according to Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem2\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem2}}}, $\tilde \frak{n}_i$ is a maximal ideal and there  exists   
an open neighbourhood $U_i = \operatorname{Spec} Q_i \subset \operatorname{Spec} R$  of $\tilde\frak{n}_i$  such that $\tilde \frak{n}_j \notin U_i$ if $j\ne i$. 
There exists also an  open neigbourhood  $\operatorname{Spec} A_i \subset \operatorname{Spec} A$ of $\frak{p}$ 
such that $Q_i/IQ_i$ is finite over $A_i$. 
Moreover,
$\frak{m}_i \hat Q_i(\frak{p})$ is the only maximal ideal of $\hat Q_i(\frak{p})$. For $\frak{q} \in \operatorname{Spec} A_i$ the ring $\hat Q_i(\frak{q})$ is semi-local with maximal ideals, say, $\frak{n}_{i,1},...,\frak{n}_{i,t}$,
and $\hat M_{Q_i} := M\hat \otimes_R Q_i$ satisfies $\hat M_{Q_i} (\frak{p}) = \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}$. We have
$$\dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i} \geq
\sum_{j=1}^t \dim_{k(\frak{q})} \hat M_{Q_i}(\frak{q})_{\frak{n}_{i,j}}
 \ \text {for all } \frak{q} \in U_i.$$
}
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{cor.scont}
Let $A$ be a Noetherian ring, $R = A[[x]]=A[x]^{\langle x \rangle}$, $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_n),$ and $M$ a finitely generated $R$-module.
 Then, for every $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$, 
the ideal $\frak{n}_\frak{p} :=  \langle \frak{p}, x_1,...,x_n\rangle_{R} $ is a prime ideal of $R$ and 
 the completed fiber w.r.t. $I=\langle x \rangle$ is the local ring  $\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong (R_{\frak{n}_\frak{p}}/\frak{p} R_{\frak{n}_\frak{p}})^\wedge\cong k(\frak{p})[[x]] $. Moreover, $\hat M(\frak{p}) \cong (M_{\frak{n}_\frak{p}}/\frak{p} M_{\frak{n}_\frak{p}})^\wedge$ and the function $\frak{p} \mapsto \dim _{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})$ is semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
We note that $\frak{n}_\frak{p} R_\frak{p}$  
is a maximal ideal of $R_\frak{p}$ and that
 $R(\frak{p})^\wedge  \cong (R_{\frak{n}_\frak{p}}/\frak{p} R_{\frak{n}_\frak{p}})^\wedge$.
This  follows from Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.2. and  {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.3..
 The semicontinuity follows from Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.scont\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.scont}}}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def.sing}
Let $A$ be a Noetherian ring.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em If  $A$ is local with maximal ideal $\frak{m}$, then  $A$ is  called {\em regular} if 
$\dim A$ is equal to its embedding dimension $\dim_{A/\frak{m}} \frak{m}/\frak{m}^2$. 
\@par\hangindent 2em $A$ is called {\em regular} if the localization at every prime ideal (equivalently at every maximal ideal, cf. \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Chapter 7]{Mat86}) is a regular local ring. 
                                                       
\@par\hangindent 2em We define the {\em regular locus} of $A$ as
$$\operatorname{Reg} (A):= \{\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \mid A_\frak{p} \text{ is regular}\}$$
 and the {\em singular locus} as
$$\operatorname{Sing} (A):=\{\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \mid A_\frak{p} \text{ is not regular}\}.$$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rm.sing}
\em {
(1) A Noetherian local ring $(A,\frak{m})$ is regular iﬀ its completion is regular (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.compl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.compl}}}.13). 
Moreover, if $(A,\frak{m})$ is regular, then $A_\frak{p}$ is regular for all 
$\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 19.3]{Mat86}).

(2) For arbitrary Noetherian rings $A$ the regular locus 
need not be open in $\operatorname{Spec} A$. However, 
$\operatorname{Reg} (A )$ is open if $A$ is a complete Noetherian local ring 
(\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Corollary of Theorem 30.10]{Mat86}) and then the  singular locus 
$\operatorname{Sing}(A)$ is closed in $\operatorname{Spec} A.$
}
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def.smooth}
Let  $\phi:A \longrightarrow B$ be a morphism of rings and $I$ an ideal in $B$. $\phi$ is called {\em (formally) $I$-smooth}, or
$B$ is called {\em (formally) $I$-smooth over $A$}, if for any $A$-algebra $C$ and any 
continuous\footnote{ Here we consider $B$ with the $I$-adic topology and $C/N$ with the discrete topology; $u$ is continuous if $u(I^m)=0$ for some $m$.}  
$A$-algebra homomorphism $u:B \longrightarrow C/N$, $N$ an ideal in $C$ with $N^2=0$, such that $u(I^m)=0$ for some $m$, there exist a morphism $\sigma:B\longrightarrow C$ such that $\pi\sigma=u$:
\[
\xymatrix{B \ar[r]^-{u}\ar@{-->}[dr]^\sigma & C/N\\
A \ar[u]^\phi\ar[r]_v & C\ar[u]_\pi
}
\relax
    \ifmmode
      \egroup \m@th$\hfil \egroup
      \end{trivlist} 
    \else \@badmath
    \fi
If $I=0$ then $B$ is called a {\em formally smooth} $A$-algebra.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem.ismooth}
Let $A$ be a ring, $B$ an $A$-algebra and $I$ an ideal in $B$.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em                             
Let $\widehat B$ be the  $I$-adic completion of $B$. Then the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $A\longrightarrow B$ is $I$-smooth, 
\@par\hangindent 2em $A \longrightarrow \widehat B$ is $I\widehat B$-smooth.
                                                                        
\end{enumerate}
                                                                                                
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $I' \subset B$ an ideal, $I\subset I'$. If 
$A\longrightarrow B$ is $I$-smooth, it is $I'$-smooth.
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $A\to A'$ be any ring map. If 
$A\longrightarrow B$ is $I$-smooth then 
$A'\longrightarrow B' = B\otimes_A A'$ is $I'=IB'$-smooth.\\
In particular,  if $A\to B$ is $I$-smooth and  $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$, then $A_\frak{p} \to B_\frak{p}$  is $I B_\frak{p}$-smooth, and $k(\frak{p}) \to B(\frak{p})$ is $IB(\frak{p})$-smooth.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
1. is proved in  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 3.1]{GP21} (see also \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 07ED]{Sta19}), for 2. see  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 07EE]{Sta19} and for 3,  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 07EG]{Sta19}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem.smooth}
Let $A$ be any ring.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $A[x]$ is $\langle x \rangle $-smooth over $A$. 
\@par\hangindent 2em $A[[x]]$ is $\langle x \rangle $-smooth over $A$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
(1) Define the dotted arrow simply by choosing lifts $c_i \in C$ of the elements in $C/N$ to which the elements $x_i$ map. \\
(2) Folllows from (1) and Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ismooth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ismooth}}}.1., since $A[[x]]$ is the $\langle x \rangle$-adic completion of $A[x]$.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop.greg}
Let $K$ be a field and $A$ a Noetherian $K$-algebra. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em A is geometrically regular over $K$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $A\otimes_K K'$ is  regular for every finite purely inseparable  extension field $K'$ of $K$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $A \otimes_K K'$ is regular for every extension field $K'$ of $K$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $A_\frak{m}$ is geometrically regular over $K$ for every maximal ideal (equivalently for every prime ideal)  $\frak{m}$ of $A$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $\hat A_\frak{m}$, the $\frak{m}$-adic completion of $A_\frak{m}$,  is geometrically regular over $K$ for every maximal ideal 
(equivalently for every prime ideal) $\frak{m}$ of $A$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $A_\frak{m}$ is  $\frak{m} A_\frak{m}$-smooth over $K$ for every maximal ideal (equivalently for every prime ideal) $\frak{m}$ of $A$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
We prove first the equivalence of 1. and 4.. 
 $A$ is geometrically regular iff $B:=A \otimes_K K'$ is regular for every finite field extension $K \subset K'$. 
 $B$ is regular iff $B_\frak{n}$ is regular for every prime ideal (maximal ideal) $\frak{n}$ of $B$ (cf. Definition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@def.sing\endcsname\@firstoftwo{def.sing}}}).
 Setting $\frak{p} = A\cap \frak{n}$, then $\frak{p} B \subset \frak{n}$ and $B_\frak{n} = (B_\frak{p})_{\frak{n} B_\frak{p}}$. Since $B_\frak{p}$ is regular iff 
 $(B_\frak{p})_{\frak{n}'}$  is regular for every maximal ideal
 $\frak{n}'$ of $B_\frak{p}$, and
 since all maximal ideals of $B_\frak{p}$ are of the form $\frak{n} B_\frak{p}$ for $\frak{n}$ a prime ideal of $B$ with 
 $\frak{p} = A\cap \frak{n}$ (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.2.), we get
 $$
 \begin{array}{lll}
 &\iff B \text{ is regular for all finite field extensions } K \subset K' \\
 & \iff  B_\frak{p} = A_\frak{p} \otimes_K K' \text{ is regular for all } \frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \\
 & \quad\quad \  \text{ and all finite field extensions } K \subset K' \\
& \iff A_\frak{p} \text{ is geometrically regular for all } \frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A.
 \end{array}
 $$
 This shows $1. \iff 4.$ for prime ideals of $A$. We still have to show $4. \Longrightarrow 1.$ for maximal ideals of $A$.
Let $\frak{p}$ be a maximal ideal of $A$, and $\tilde\frak{m}_1,...,\tilde \frak{m}_r$ the maximal ideals of $B$ with $\tilde\frak{m}_i=\frak{p} \cap B$ (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.3.).  Then $B_\frak{p}$ regular iff 
$(B_\frak{p})_{\tilde\frak{m}_\frak{p} B_\frak{p}}= B_{\tilde\frak{m}_\frak{p}}$ is regular for  all maximal ideals $\tilde\frak{m}_\frak{p}$ of $B$ lying over $\frak{p}$.
Since $B_\frak{p}$ is semi-local (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.3.) and  all maximal ideals of $B$ are of this form by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.3., $B$ is regular and hence $A$ is geometrically regular.

Since $A_\frak{m}$
is local with maximal ideal $\frak{m} A_\frak{m}$ for every prime ideal 
$\frak{m}$ of $A$, we may therefore assume that $A$ is local
for the equivalence of  statements 1.,2.,3. and for the equivalence of 4.,5.,6.. 
In the following let $A$ be local with maximal ideal $\frak{m}$.

 
We show  the equivalence of 4. and 5.. Let $\hat A$ be the $\frak{m}$-adic completion of $A$, and $K \subset K'$ a finite field extension. We set $B:=A \otimes_K K'$ and  $C:=\hat A \otimes_K K' = 
 (\varprojlim A/\frak{m}^k) \otimes_K K' =
 (A \otimes_K K' )^{\wedge,\frak{m} B} = B^{\wedge,\frak{m} B}$ (by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.lim\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.lim}}}).
Then $A \to B$ resp. $\hat A \to C$ are finite morphisms with fiber
$B(\frak{m}) = B/\frak{m} B=C/\frak{m} C=C(\frak{m})$ being a semi-local ring with maximal ideals
$\bar\frak{m}_1,...,\bar\frak{m}_r$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.1.. 
By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.2.
$B$ resp. $C$ are semi-local rings with maximal ideals the  preimages $\frak{n}_{i}=\bar\frak{m}_i \cap B$ resp.  $\tilde\frak{m}_{i}=\bar\frak{m}_i \cap C$ in $B$ resp. $C$, $i=1,...,r$.\\
Now we use Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}} with $I=0$, $\frak{p} = \frak{m}$, $R=R_\frak{p} = B$, and $\tilde R_\frak{p} = C$. Then 
Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.2. implies 
$C= C^{\wedge,\tilde \frak{m}_1}\oplus ... \oplus C^{\wedge,\tilde \frak{m}_r}$, with $C_{\tilde \frak{m}_i} = C^{\wedge,\tilde \frak{m}_i} = B^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i} =(B_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge,\frak{n}_i}$
complete local rings. We have that 
$$
\begin{array}{llll}
&B \text{ is regular } \iff 
B_{\frak{n}_i} \text{ is regular for all  $i$}  \iff (B_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge, \frak{n}_i} \text{ is regular for all $i$},\\
&C\text{ is regular }  \iff 
C_{\tilde\frak{m}_i} = (B_{\frak{n}_i})^{\wedge, \frak{n}_i} \text{ is regular for all  $i$}. 
\end{array}
$$
Hence $B$ is regular $\iff$ $C$ is regular. Since the
field extension $K \subset K'$ is arbitrary, it follows that
$B$ is geometrically regular iff $C$ is geometrically regular, which proves $4. \iff 5.$.


It remains to prove the equivalence of statements 1.,2.,3.,6.
with $(A,\frak{m})$ local.
The equivalence of 1. and 2. is proved in \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 0381]{Sta19}, the equivalence of 1. and 6. in \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 28.7 ]{Mat86}. 

Since 3. $\Rightarrow$ 1. is trivial, let us prove 1. 
$\Rightarrow$ 3.
 Assume 1. and let $K'$ be any field extension of $K$. 
By 1. $\Rightarrow$ 6. $A$ is  $\frak{m}$-smooth over $K$ and
