from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ismooth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ismooth}}}.3. we get that the (semi-local) $K'$-algebra
$B=:A \otimes_K K'$ is $\frak{m} B$-smooth over $K'$.  
Again by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ismooth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ismooth}}}.3. the local ring
$B_\frak{n}$ is $\frak{m} B_\frak{n}$-smooth
for every prime ideal (maximal ideal) $\frak{n}$ of $B$. By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.2. $\frak{m} B \subset \frak{n}$ and by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.ismooth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.ismooth}}}.2. we have that $B_\frak{n}$ is $\frak{n} B_\frak{n}$-smooth over $K'$. By
6. $\Rightarrow$ 1. implies that
$B_\frak{n}$ is geometrically regular over $K'$, in particular a regular ring. Hence $B=A\otimes_K K'$ is regular, showing 3..
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{rm.reg}
Let $(A,\frak{m})$ be a local Noetherian ring containing a field $K$. 
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $A/\frak{m}$ be separable over $K$. Then 
$$A \text{ is  $\frak{m}$-smooth over  $K$}  \Leftrightarrow \text{$A$ is geometrically regular over $K$} \Leftrightarrow  \text{$A$ is regular.}$$
\@par\hangindent 2em Let $K'$ be a finite field extension of $K$. Then $A'=A\otimes_K K'$ is a semi-local ring.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
1. follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 28.7 and Lemma 1, page 216]{Mat86}.\\
2. Since $A\to A'$ is finite , $A'= A'_\frak{m}$ is semi-local by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.sem0}}}.2.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- example-7: body_md -->
{\em
We show that finiteness of the field extension is necessary.
Let $t$ be a variable, $S=\mathbbm{Q}[t]\setminus \{0\}$ then $\mathbbm{Q}(t)\otimes_{\mathbbm{Q}} \mathbbm{C}=S^{-1}\mathbbm{C}[t]$. $\langle t-\alpha\rangle \cap S=\emptyset$ iff $\alpha$ is transzendental over $\mathbbm{Q}$.  This implies that $\mathbbm{Q}(t)\otimes_{\mathbbm{Q}} \mathbbm{C}$ has uncountably many maximal ideals, in particular, it is not semi-local.}
<!-- end example-7 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem.reg.fieldextension}
Let $K$ be a field and $K'$ be a separable algebraic field extension of $K$. Let $A$ be a $K'$-algebra.
$A$ is geometrically regular over $K$ if and only if $A$ is geometrically regular over $K'$.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
The lemma is proved in \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[tag 07QH]{Sta19}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- example-8: body_md -->
\label{ex.reg.fieldextension}{\em
We show that the separability of the field extension is necessary in the above lemma.
Let $K$ be a field of characteristic $p>0$. Let $K:=K(t^p)\subset  K(t)=:K'$ for an element $t$ transcendental over $K$, which is a non-separable extension. 
Let $z:=t\otimes 1 - 1\otimes t\in K'\otimes_KK'$. Then $z\neq 0$ but $z^p=0$. Let $A:=K'[[x]]$ then we obtain from lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.smooth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.smooth}}} and proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.greg\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.greg}}} that $A$ is geometrically regular over $K'$. Now the canonical map $K'\otimes_KK'\longrightarrow K'\otimes_K A$ is injective since the canonical injection $i:K'\longrightarrow A$ is a split map\footnote{The projection $p:A\longrightarrow K'$ defined by $p(\sum_{i\geq 0}a_ix^i)=a_0$ is $K$-linear and $p\circ i=id_{K'}$. The corresponding projection induced by the tensor product $p'=id_{K'}\otimes p :K'\otimes_K A\longrightarrow k'\otimes_KK'$ is also $K$-linear and $p'\circ i'=id_{K'\otimes_KK'}$ with $i'=id_{K'}\otimes i$.}. This implies that $z$ considered as element in $K'\otimes_K A$ is a non-zero nilpotent element and therefore $A$ is not geometrically regular over $K$.}
<!-- end example-8 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm.Groth}
Let $(A,\frak{m}) \to (B,\frak{n})$ be a morphism of Noetherian local rings. Let $\bar B=B/\frak{m} B$ and $\bar \frak{n}=\frak{n}/\frak{m} B$. Then $B$ is $\frak{n}$-smooth over $A$ iff $\bar B$ is $\bar \frak{n}$-smooth (geometrically regular) over $A/\frak{m}$ and $B$ is flat over $A$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem.jac}
Let $K$ be any field and $J\subset \langle x \rangle$ an ideal of $K[[x]]$.  If $\dim K[[x]]/J=d$, the following are equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $K[[x]]/J$ is $\langle x \rangle$-smooth over $K$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $K[[x]]/J$ is a regular local ring. 
\@par\hangindent 2em $K[[x]]/J$ is geometrically regular over $K$.
\@par\hangindent 2em $K[[x]]/J \cong K[[y_1,\dots,y_d]].$ 
\@par\hangindent 2em $I_d(Jac (J)) = K[[x]]$ {\em (Jacobian criterion)}.\\
In particular, if $J=\langle f\rangle$ is a principal ideal then
$K[[x]]/\langle f\rangle$ is regular iff the partial derivatives of $f$ generate $ K[[x]]$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
The equivalence of 1., 2. and 3. follow from Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.reg\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.reg}}}.2.
($K$ coincides with the residue field of $K[[x]]/J$),
the equivalence of 2. and 4.  from Cohen's structure theorem (Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.cohen\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.cohen}}}) below. 

 For the Jacobian criterion let $J=\langle f_1,...,f_m \rangle$
 and we note that $n-d\le m$ (by \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem B.2.1.]{GLS07}).
That  4. implies  5. follows from the definition of $I_n(Jac (J))$, while 
5. implies 4. is a consequence of the implicit mapping theorem for formal power series:
$I_d(Jac (J)) = K[[x]]$ is equivalent to $I_d(Jac (J))$ containing
a unit of $K[[x]]$, which is equivalent to the existence of an $(n-d)$-minor of $Jac(J)|_{x=0}$, which is non-zero in $K$. By \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem I.1.18]{GLS07} we get $K[[x]]/J\cong K[[y_1,\dots,y_d]]$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm.cohen}
Let $(R,\frak{m})$ be a Noetherian complete local ring, containing a field\,\footnote{Also called the equal characteristic case. Then $R$ contains a {\em coefficient field  K},  i.e. $K$ maps isomorphically to $k(\frak{m})$
under $R\to R/\frak{m}$.} and $k(\frak{m}) = R/\frak{m}$ its residue field. Then
$$R \cong k(\frak{m})[[x_1,...,x_n]]/J,$$
for some ideal $J$. If $\frak{m} = \langle m_1,...,m_n\rangle$ and
$\phi : k(\frak{m})[[x_1,...,x_n]] \to R$ the map $x_i \mapsto m_i$, then $\phi$ is surjective and $J = \ker(\phi)$.\\
If $R$ is regular of dimension $d$, then 
$$R \cong k(\frak{m}) [[x_1,...,x_d]].$$
For a proof see see \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Lemma 10.155.10, tag 0C0S]{Sta19} and \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 6.3.10]{GP08}.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem.slocus}
Let $B:= K[[x]]/J$, $J$ an ideal in $\langle x \rangle K[[x]]$.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em If  $B$ is pure $d$-dimensional (i.e. $\dim B/P =d$ for all minimal primes $P \in \operatorname{Spec} B$), then
$$\operatorname{Sing} (B) = V(I_d(Jac(J))\cdot B) \subset \operatorname{Spec} B,$$
which we identify with $V(I+I_d(Jac(J)) \subset \operatorname{Spec} K[[x]].$
\@par\hangindent 2em If $B$ is not pure dimensional, let $P_1,\dots,P_r$ be the minimal primes  of $B$. Then $B/P_i$ is pure dimensional and then  
$$\operatorname{Sing} (B) = \bigcup_{i=1}^r \operatorname{Sing} (B/P_i) \ \cup  \ \bigcup_{i \ne j}  V(P_i)\cap V(P_j)\subset \operatorname{Spec} B.$$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{def.iso}
We say that  $K[[x]]/J$ (or $J$) has an {\em isolated singularity} (at 0) if the maximal ideal  $\langle x \rangle$ is an isolated point of $\operatorname{Sing}(K[[x]]/J)$ or if $K[[x]]/J$ is regular, i.e. 
 $\operatorname{Sing}(K[[x]]/J) =\{\langle x \rangle\}$ or $\emptyset$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Let $P\in \operatorname{Spec} B$ and $B_P$ regular of dimension $d$. Then the
completion $\hat B_P$ is regular of dimension $d$ (Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sing\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sing}}}) and  $\hat B_P \cong k(P)[[x_1,...,x_d]]$ by Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.cohen\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.cohen}}}.
From Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.jac\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.jac}}}.3 we see that $\hat B_P$ and hence  $B_P= K[[x]]_P/J_P$ is irreducible and from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.jac\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.jac}}}.4 that $P \notin V(I_d(Jac(J)))$.

Conversely, let $P\notin \bigcup_{i=1}^r \operatorname{Sing} (B/P_i) \ \cup  \ \bigcup_{i \ne j}  V(P_i)\cap V(P_j)$. Then there exists a minmal prime $P_i$ of $B$ such that $P \in V(P_i)$ and 
$P \notin V(P_j)$ for $j\ne i$. Then $B_P$ is
pure $d$-dimensional, $d= \dim B/P_i$, and $P\notin \operatorname{Sing} (B/P_i)$, i.e. $P\notin V(I_d(K[[x]]/P_i))$. Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.jac\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.jac}}} implies that $K[[x]]/P_i$ is regular. Hence $B_P = (K[[x]]/P_i)_P$ is regular by Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sing\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sing}}}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{def.smooth1}
 Let $\phi: A \to R$ be a morphism of Noetherian rings.  $\phi$ is called {\em flat} (resp. {\em smooth}) at $ \frak{n}\in \operatorname{Spec} R$,  if the induced morphism of local rings,
 $\phi_{\frak{n}}: A_\frak{p} \to R_\frak{n}$,  $\frak{p} := A\cap \frak{n}$,
 is flat (resp. $ \frak{n} R_{ \frak{n}}$-smooth). $\phi$ is called  flat,  (resp. smooth) if this holds at every $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$. 
We denote the {\em smooth locus} of $\phi$ by
 $$\operatorname{Sm}(\phi) := \{\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R \mid \phi \text{ is smooth at } \frak{n}\}$$
 and by 
 $$\operatorname{NSm}(\phi) := \{\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R \mid \phi \text{ is not smooth at } \frak{n}\}$$
 the {\em non-smooth locus} of $\phi$.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{rm.sopen}{\em
(1)  By Grothendieck (Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.Groth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.Groth}}}) $\phi$ is smooth at $\frak{n}$ iff 
$\phi$ is flat at $\frak{n}$ and  if the fiber $R(\frak{p})_\frak{n} = R_\frak{n}(\frak{p})=R_\frak{n}\otimes_{A_\frak{p}}k(\frak{p})$ of $\phi_{\frak{n}}$ is  $\frak{n} R_\frak{n}(\frak{p})$-smooth (equivalently geometrically regular, by Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.greg\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.greg}}}) over $k(\frak{p})$. 

(2) In general the smooth locus may not be open, but if $\phi: A \to R$ is of finite presentation  (i.e. $R \cong A[x_1, \ldots, x_n]/\langle f_1, \ldots, f_m\rangle$)
then $\operatorname{Sm} (\phi)$ is open in $\operatorname{Spec} R$. This follows since the flat locus as well as the locus, where the fiber is geometrically regular, are open by by \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[IV$_3$, Theorem 11.1.1]{EGA} and  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[IV$_3$, Theorem 12.1.6]{EGA}. }
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- notation-1: body_md -->
\label{rm.notation}{\em
Now  let $I \subset R$ be an ideal such that $R/I$ is finite over $A$. 
 For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ the completed fiber
$(R_\frak{p}/\frak{p} R_\frak{p})^{\wedge,I}=\hat R(\frak{p})$ is a semi-local $k(\frak{p})$-algebra by Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}. To make the dependence on $\frak{p}$ visible, we denote its maximal ideals by 
$\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1},...,\frak{m}_{\frak{p},r_\frak{p}}$ instead of $\frak{m}_{1},...,\frak{m}_{r}$
(and similar with the ideals $\bar \frak{m}_i$ and the other ideals derived from it).
Then
 $$\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong   \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1}}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},r_\frak{p}}}$$
 is semi-local  with $\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}}$ a complete local ring for each $i=1,...,r_\frak{p}$, which contains the residue field $k(\frak{p})$ of $A_\frak{p}$. By Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.cohen\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.cohen}}}
$$\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}} \cong k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})[[x_1,...,x_n]]/J_{\frak{p},i}$$
  for some ideal $J_{\frak{p},i}$, and the residue field $k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})$ of $\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}}$ is  finite over $k(\frak{p})$, but not necessarily separable as the following example shows.}
<!-- end notation-1 -->
<!-- example-9: body_md -->
\label{ex.nsep}{\em
Let $K$ be a field of characteristic $p>0$, $A=K[t]$, $P=A[x]$,
$I=\langle x^p -t\rangle$ and $R=P^{\wedge,I}$. 
For $\frak{p}=\langle 0 \rangle \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ we have
$$ \hat R(\frak{p}) = K(t)[x]^{\wedge,I}. $$
This is a complete local ring with maximal ideal 
$\frak{m} = \langle x^p -t\rangle  \hat R(\frak{p})$ (by Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}) and residue field 
$k(\frak{m}) =  K(t)[x]/ \langle x^p -t\rangle = K(\sqrt[p]{t})$, which is not separable over $K(t)$. By Cohen we have $ \hat R(\frak{p}) \cong  K(\sqrt[p]{t})[[x]]$.}
<!-- end example-9 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop.reg}
Let $A\to R$ be a morphism of Noetherian rings,  
$I\subset R$ an ideal,
$F_1,...,F_m$ elements of $R$, and $M$ a finitely generated $R$-module. 
Let  ${}^\wedge$ denote  the $I$-adic completion. 
For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ let 
$F_i(\frak{p})$ resp. $\hat F_i(\frak{p})$ denote the image of $F_i$ 
in $R(\frak{p}) = R\otimes_A k(\frak{p})$ resp. in $\hat R(\frak{p}) = R(\frak{p})^{\wedge}$.

 \begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em [(i)] 
The following are equivalent.
 \begin{enumerate}
 \@par\hangindent 2em   
 $M/ \langle F_1,..., F_m\rangle M$  is flat over $A$ and $F_1,\ldots,F_m$  is an $M$-regular sequence.
 \@par\hangindent 2em $M$ is flat over $A$ and for every $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(M)$, $F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,F_m(\frak{p})$  is an $M(\frak{p})$-regular sequence.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, condition (a) implies that $M/\langle F_1,...,F_i\rangle M$, $i=0,...,m$, is flat over $A$.
\@par\hangindent 2em [(ii)] 
The following conditions {\em (a)} and  {\em (b)} are equivalent and imply  {\em (c)}.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em$M^\wedge/ \langle F_1,..., F_m\rangle M^\wedge$ is flat over $A$ and $F_1,\ldots,F_m$  is an $M^\wedge$-regular sequence.
\@par\hangindent 2em $M^\wedge$ is flat over $A$ and and for every $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(M^\wedge)$,  $F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,F_m(\frak{p})$  is an $M^\wedge(\frak{p})$-regular sequence. 
\@par\hangindent 2em  $M^\wedge$ is flat over $A$ and and for every $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(M^\wedge)$,  $\hat F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{p})$  is a 
$\hat M(\frak{p})$-regular sequence. 
 \end{enumerate}
 Moreover, statement {\em (a)} of {\em (i)} implies statement  {\em (a)} of {\em (ii)}. 
 \smallskip
 
 \@par\hangindent 2em [(iii)] 
 Let  $I\subset R$ be an ideal with $R/I$ finite over $A$
 and $F_{1},\ldots,F_{m} \in I$. Let $M$ and $M/\langle F_{1},\ldots,F_{m}\rangle M$ be $A$-flat and let the induced map $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM) \to \operatorname{Spec} A$ be open.\footnote{E.g. if $M/IM$ is flat over $A$. Since $A\to R/I$ is finite,
 $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM) \to \operatorname{Spec} A$ being open is equivalent to $A\to R/\operatorname{Ann}_R(M/IM)$ has the going-down property.}
For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(M/IM)$ let
  $\hat F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{p})$  be an $\hat M(\frak{p})$-sequence. 
Then there exists an  open neighbourhood $U$ of \ $\frak{p}$  in $\operatorname{Spec} A$ such that 
 $\hat F_1(\frak{q}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{q})$ is a $\hat M(\frak{q})$-sequence for all $\frak{q}$ in $U$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
(i) Set $M_0=M,  M_i=M/\langle F_{1},\ldots,F_{i}\rangle M$ and  consider for 
$i=1,\ldots, m$ the exact sequence 
\begin{equation}\label{eq.flat}
	0 \to K_{i-1} \to M_{i-1}  \xrightarrow[\text {}]{F_i} M_{i-1} \to M_i \to 0,  
\end{equation}
with $K_{i-1}$ the kernel of $F_i$. Then $F_1,\ldots, F_m $ is an $M$-regular sequence iff $K_{i-1}=0$ for $i=1,...,m$. 
The sequence  ({{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@eq.flat\endcsname\@firstoftwo{eq.flat}}}) stays exact if we localize 
it at a prime ideal $\frak{n}$ of $R$. Now $K_{i-1}=0$ iff
$(K_{i-1})_\frak{n}=0$ for all prime (equivalently maximal) ideals $\frak{n}$ in $R$ and, by \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 7.1]{Mat86},   $M$ is $A$-flat iff $M_\frak{n}$ is $A_{A\cap \frak{n}}$-flat for all prime (equivalently maximal) ideals $\frak{n}$ in $R$.
Hence (a) equivalent to:

($\alpha$) $M_\frak{n}/ \langle F_1,..., F_m\rangle M_\frak{n}$  is flat over $A_{A\cap \frak{n}}$ and $F_1,\ldots,F_m$  is an $M_\frak{n}$-regular sequence for all prime (equivalently maximal) ideals $\frak{n}$ of $R$.
 
\noindent 
If we fix a prime ideal $\frak{n}$ of $R$ and set $\frak{p} = A\cap \frak{n}$ then, by successive application of  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 22.5]{Mat86}
applied to $A_\frak{p} \to R_\frak{n}$  and  starting with $F_m$, 
statement ($\alpha$)
is equivalent to:
 
($\beta$) $(M_i)_\frak{n}$ is flat over $A_\frak{p}$ for $i=0,...,m$, 
and  $F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,F_m(\frak{p})$  is an $M(\frak{p})$-regular sequence. 
 
 \noindent If $\frak{n}$ runs through all maximal ideals of 
$\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM)$, then $\frak{p}=A\cap \frak{n}$ runs through all prime ideals of 
$\operatorname{Supp}_A(M)$, since $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM)\to \operatorname{Supp}_A(M/I)$ is surjective (see Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sem0}}}). This proves the equivalence of (a) and (b).\\

(ii)  Let us first show that (i)(a) implies (ii)(a). We have
$L^{\wedge} = L\otimes_R R^{\wedge}$ for any finite $R$-module $L$ by Corollary {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@cor.cpl\endcsname\@firstoftwo{cor.cpl}}} (iv). Since $R^{\wedge}$ is flat over $R$, the flatness of $M^\wedge/ \langle F_1,..., F_m\rangle M^\wedge$  follows hence from the flatness of
$M/ \langle F_1,..., F_m\rangle M$.
The $I$-adic completion of sequence ({{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@eq.flat\endcsname\@firstoftwo{eq.flat}}}),
\begin{equation}\label{eq.flat1}
	0 \to K_{i-1}^\wedge \to M_{i-1}^\wedge  \xrightarrow[\text {}]{F_i}  M_{i-1}^\wedge \to  M_i^\wedge \to 0,  
\end{equation}
is an exact sequence of $R^\wedge$-modules and since
 $K^\wedge_{i-1} =0$ if $K_{i-1} =0$, it follows that
 $F_1,\ldots, F_m $ is an $M^\wedge$-regular sequence.
The equivalence of (a) and (b) in (ii) follows by applying (i) to $M^\wedge$.

To show (b) $\Rightarrow$ (c), we use Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.pctp\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.pctp}}}.1. and get $\hat M(\frak{p}) = M(\frak{p})^\wedge =
(M^\wedge(\frak{p}))^\wedge = (M^\wedge(\frak{p}))\otimes_{R(\frak{p})} \hat R(\frak{p})$.
Since $\hat R(\frak{p})$ is flat over $R(\frak{p})$,
it follows that $\hat F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{p})$  is $\hat M(\frak{p})$-regular. \\

(iii) We have to show, that for $i=1,...,m$  the kernel of the (by $F_i$ induced) map
$$\hat F_i(\frak{q}):\hat M_{i-1}(\frak{q}) \to \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{q})$$
vanishes and that $\hat M_m(\frak{q})\ne 0$
for $\frak{q}$ in some  neighbourhood $U$ of  the given $\frak{p}\in \operatorname{Supp}_A(M/I)$. \\

 Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.open\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.open}}}.2. and Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sem0}}}
  say that for any finite $R$-module $M$ and $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$,
$\hat M(\frak{p}) = M(\frak{p})^\wedge \ne 0$ iff 
$\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A (M/IM)$ and that the natural map ($\frak{n} \mapsto \frak{n} \cap A$), 
 $$\phi: \operatorname{Supp}_R (M/IM) \to \operatorname{Supp}_A (M/IM)
 = \{\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \mid \hat M(\frak{p}) \ne 0\},$$ 
is finite and surjective. Moreover, if $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq.5}
\hat M(\frak{p})_\frak{n} \ne 0 \iff \frak{n} \in \operatorname{Supp}_R(M)\cap V(I),
\ \frak{p} = \frak{n} \cap A.
\end{equation}


Since $F_1,...,F_m \in I$ it follows that
$\operatorname{Supp}_R (M/IM)
=  \operatorname{Supp}_R (M) \cap V(I)= \operatorname{Supp}_R (M)\cap V(I+\langle F_1,...,F_i\rangle)= \operatorname{Supp}_R(M_i/IM_i)$ 
for $i=1,...,m$.
In particular $\hat M(\frak{p}) \ne 0 \iff \hat M_i(\frak{p}) \ne 0$
for one (equivalently for all) $i=1,...,m$. \\

By Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.4. (and Notation {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.notation\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.notation}}}), 
$\hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p}) =  \oplus_{j=1}^{r_\frak{p}} \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}}$
and, accordingly,  the map
 $\hat F_i(\frak{p}): \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p}) \to \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p}) $ splits as a direct sum
$$\hat F_i(\frak{p}):\oplus_{j=1}^{r_\frak{p}} \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}}\xrightarrow[\text {}]{\oplus_j \hat F_{i,j}(\frak{p})}  \oplus_{j=1}^{r_\frak{p}} \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}}.$$
By assumption the $\hat F_i(\frak{p})$ and hence the maps $\hat F_{i,j}(\frak{p}) : \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}} \to \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}}$ are injective and 
$\hat M_{m}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}}\ne 0$ for some $j=j_0$. 
Furthermore 
$$\hat M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j}} \cong
(M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}}
= M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}\otimes_{R_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}} (R_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}}.$$
As $(R_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}})^{\wedge,{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}}$ 
is faithfully flat over the local ring $R_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}$, it follows that the map 
$$F_{i,j}(\frak{p}): M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}} \to M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}$$
 is injective for all $i=1,...,m, \ j=1,...,r_\frak{p}$
 and $M_{m}(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},j_0}}\ne 0$. 
 
 We  apply now repeatedly \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 22.5, (2) $\Rightarrow$ (1)]{Mat86},  to the morphism of local rings
$(A_\frak{p},k(\frak{p})) \to (R_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}},k({\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}})$ 
and to the map
$$F_{i,\frak{p},j}: (M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}} \to (M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}},$$
which satisfies
$$F_{i,j}(\frak{p}) = F_{i,\frak{p},j}\otimes_Ak(\frak{p}): (M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}\otimes_Ak(\frak{p})=M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}\to M_{i-1}(\frak{p})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}.$$
 Starting with $i=1$ ($M$ is  $A$-flat  by assumption, hence
 $M_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}$ is $A_\frak{p}$-flat)  we get
that the kernel 
$(K_{i-1})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}$ of $F_{i,\frak{p},j}$
vanishes 
and that 
$(M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}/F_{i,j}(M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}=(M_{i})_{\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}}$ is flat over $A_\frak{p}$ for all $i,j$.\\

The support of the $R$-module $K_{i-1}$ is closed and hence
 $(K_{i-1})_{\frak{n}}=0$  for $\frak{n}$ in an open neighbourhood   $U_{i-1,j}$  of $\frak{n}_{\frak{p},j}$ in $\operatorname{Spec} R$.
 That is,
$F_{i,j}: (M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}} \to (M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}}$ is injective for
all $\frak{n} \in U_{i-1,j}$, in particular for all $\frak{n} \in V_{i-1,j}:=U_{i-1,j} \cap \operatorname{Supp}_R (M/IM)$. 
Since $M_m$ is $A$-flat
by assumption,
$(M_{m})_\frak{n}$ is $A_{\frak{n} \cap A}$-flat for all $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Supp}_R(M_m) \supset \operatorname{Supp}_R (M/IM)$. 
It follows  from  \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Corollary to Theorem 22.5, (1) $\Rightarrow$ (2)]{Mat86} that 
$$F_{i,j}(\frak{q}): (M_{i-1})_{\frak{n}}\otimes_A k(\frak{q})= M_{i-1}(\frak{q})_{\frak{n}}\to M_{i-1}(\frak{q})_{\frak{n}},  
\ \frak{q} = \frak{n}\cap A,$$
 is injective for all $\frak{n} \in V_{i-1,j}$.
 
 Set $V_{i-1}:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{r_\frak{p}}V_{i-1,j}$. 
 Then $V_{i-1}$ is open in $\operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM)$
 and
 disjoint from $\operatorname{Supp}_R(K_{i-1})$. 
Moreover, $\phi(V_{i-1})$ is disjoint from $\phi(\operatorname{Supp}_R(K_{i-1}))$. To see this, assume that there exists a
 $\frak{q} \in  \phi(\operatorname{Supp}_R(K_{i-1}))$ and an   $\frak{n} \in V_{i-1}$ such that $\phi(\frak{n}) = \frak{q}$. By ({{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@eq.5\endcsname\@firstoftwo{eq.5}}})
 $\hat K_{i-1}(\frak{q})_\frak{n} = ((K_{i-1}(\frak{q}))_\frak{n})^\wedge = 
 ((K_{i-1})_\frak{n}(\frak{q}))^\wedge  \ne 0$ iff 
 $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Supp}_R (K_{i-1}) \cap V(I)$. This is a contradiction since $(K_{i-1})_\frak{n} =0$ if $\frak{n} \in V_{i-1}$.\\
  
It follows that
  $F_i(\frak{q}): M_{i-1}(\frak{q}) \to  M_{i-1}(\frak{q})$  and,  since $\hat R(\frak{q})$ is  flat over  $R(\frak{q})$,
 $\hat F_i(\frak{q}): \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{q})=M_{i-1}(\frak{q})\otimes_{R(\frak{q})}\hat R(\frak{q})
   \to \hat M_{i-1}(\frak{q})$
 is injective for $\frak{q} \in U:=\phi (V_{i-1})$ and $i=1,...,m$. Since $\phi$ is open by assumption, $U$ is open in $\operatorname{Spec} A$. Furthermore, if $\frak{n} \in V_{i-1}$, then
 $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Supp}_R(M/IM) = \operatorname{Supp}_R (M_m)\cap V(I)$.
 Hence $\hat M_m(\frak{q}) \ne 0$  by Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sem0}}}
 for all $\frak{q} \in U$. This proves (iii).
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{def.mutau}
For $f\in \langle x \rangle K[[x]]$ we define
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em[(i)] the {\em Milnor number} of $f$ (w.r.t. $K$), 
$$ \mu_K (f):= \dim_K K[[x]]/j(f)$$

\@par\hangindent 2em[(ii)] and  the {\em Tjurina number} of $f$ (w.r.t. $K$), 
$$ \tau_K (f):= \dim_K  K[[x]]/\langle f,j(f)\rangle.$$

\@par\hangindent 2em[(iii)] We say that $f$ resp. $K[[x]]/\langle f\rangle$   has an {\em isolated singularity} (w.r.t. $K$) if $\mu_K(f) < \infty$
resp.  $\tau_K(f) < \infty$. 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{rem.mutau} {\em
To shorten notations we set $R:=K[[x]]$ and $A:=K[[t]]$.
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em [(1)]   We have always  $\tau_K(f) \leq \mu_K(f)$  and
  $\tau_K(f) = \mu_K(f)  \iff f \in j(f)$. Moroever,
$\mu_K(f) < \infty$
resp.  $\tau_K(f) < \infty$ iff $\langle x \rangle^k \subset j(f)$ resp. $\langle x \rangle^k \subset \langle f,j(f)\rangle$ for some $k\ge 0$. 
Let us call 
$$V(j(f))=  \{\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} K[[x]] \mid {\partial f}/{\partial x_i} \in \frak{n}, \ i=1,...,n \}$$
the {\em singular locus of the power series} $f$.
By Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.slocus\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.slocus}}} the {\em singular locus of the local algebra}  $K[[x]]/\langle f \rangle)$ is 
$$\operatorname{Sing} (K[[x]]/\langle f \rangle) = V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle).$$ 
Hence $\mu_K(f) < \infty$ 
(resp. $\tau_K(f) < \infty$) iff  $\operatorname{Sing} (f)$ (resp. $\operatorname{Sing} (K[[x]]/\langle f \rangle)$ is either $\{\langle x \rangle\}$ or $\emptyset$. 

\@par\hangindent 2em [(2)] Consider the map 
$\phi: A=K[[t]] \to R=K[[x]]$, $\phi(t) = f$, 
and the induced map
$\operatorname{Spec} \phi: \operatorname{Spec} R \to \operatorname{Spec} A$.
For $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$ set  $\frak{p} =\frak{p}_\frak{n} =(\operatorname{Spec} \phi) (\frak{n})$, i.e. $\frak{p} =A \cap \frak{n}= \phi^{-1}(\frak{n})$.
Since $\phi$ is flat, its non-smooth locus of $\phi$ satisfies (cf. Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sopen\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sopen}}})
\begin{center}
$\operatorname{NSm}(\phi) = \{\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R \mid R(\frak{p})_\frak{n} \text{ is not geometrically regular over } k(\frak{p}) \}.$
\end{center}

\@par\hangindent 2em [(3)] Let $\operatorname{char}(K) = 0$. 
Then the  singular locus of $f$
 coincides with the singular locus of $K[[x]]/\langle f \rangle$ and with the non-smooth locus of $\phi$ i.e. 
$$V(j(f)) = V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle) = \operatorname{NSm}(\phi).$$

\noindent In fact,  in characteristic 0 we  have $V(j(f)) \subset V(f)$.
This follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 7.1.5]{SH06} which implies that $f$ is contained in the integral closure and hence in the radical of $j(f)$. Therefore $f^k \in j(f)$ for some $k$ and thus 
$V(j(f)) \subset V(f)$.
The Brian\c{c}on-Skoda theorem \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 13.3.3]{SH06} implies that $j(f)^n$ is contained in the integral closure of $j(f)$, hence even $f^n \in j(f)$. This proves the first equality, the second follows from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.smphi\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.smphi}}}.\\
It is proved in \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Theorem 2]{BGM12} that
 $\mu(f) < \infty \Leftrightarrow \tau(f) < \infty$,  but the example $f=x^3+y^5$ shows that this is not true in characteristic 3 and 5.
 
\@par\hangindent 2em [(4)] Let $\operatorname{char}(K) = p >0$. Then 
$$V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle) \subset  \operatorname{NSm}(\phi) \text{ and }V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle)  \subset  V(j(f)),$$
 and in general the inclusions are strict. The first inclusion follows from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.smphi\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.smphi}}} with equality if $K$ is perfect.
The second inclusion is clear. 
For $f=x^3+y^5$, $p=3,5$, we have $V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle) = V(x,y)$, which is strictly contained in $V(j(f)) = V(y)$ (even if $K$ is perfect).
\end{enumerate}
}
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem.smphi}
Let $K$ be a field, 
$f\in K[[x]]$ and
$\phi: A=K[[t]] \to R=K[[x]]$, $\phi(t) = f$.
Then $ V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle)$ is contained in  the 
 non-smooth locus $\operatorname{NSm}(\phi)$ of $\phi$, 
 with equality if $K$ is perfect.\\
More precisely for  $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$ the following holds. 

\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em  If  $\operatorname{Spec} \phi)(\frak{n}) = \langle 0\rangle$
 then $R(\langle 0\rangle)_\frak{n}$ is geometrically regular over $K$ and hence also over the extension field $k(\langle 0\rangle)$.That is, the generic fiber $R(\langle 0\rangle)$ 
is geometrically regular over 
$k(\langle 0\rangle)$ and hence $\operatorname{Spec} R(\langle 0\rangle) \subset\operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$.

\@par\hangindent 2em 
Let $(\operatorname{Spec} \phi)(\frak{n}) = \langle t \rangle$ and  $R(\langle t \rangle) = R/\langle f\rangle$ 
 the special fiber.
If $\frak{n}\in  V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle)$ 
then  $R(\langle t \rangle)_\frak{n}$ is not geometrically regular over $k(\langle t \rangle) = K$ and hence $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{NSm}(\phi)$. \\
If $\frak{n}\notin  V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle)$ then $R_\frak{n}/\langle f\rangle$ is geometrically regular over $k(\frak{n})$
(but not necessarily over $k(\langle t \rangle) = K$  if $\frak{n} \ne \langle x \rangle$;  if $\frak{n} = \langle x \rangle$ then $k(\frak{n}) =K$). If $K$ is perfect, then $R(\langle t\rangle)_\frak{n}$ is  geometrically regular over $K$ and hence $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$.
\end {enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
1. That  $R(\langle 0\rangle)_\frak{n}=K[[x]]_\frak{n}$ is
geometrically regular over $K$ follows from Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.smooth\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.smooth}}}, Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.jac\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.jac}}} and Proposition  {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.greg\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.greg}}} and hence $\operatorname{Spec} R(\langle 0\rangle) \subset
\operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$.

2. By 1. $\operatorname{NSm}(\phi) \subset V(f)$. Let $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Spec} R$ and $\frak{n} \in V(f) \cap \operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$. We want to show that $\frak{n} \notin V(j(f))$, i.e. $j(f)\not\subset \frak{n}$. We note that $j(f) \subset \frak{n}$ implies that $j(f)R_\frak{n} \subset \frak{n} R_\frak{n}$
 and hence that $j(f)(R_\frak{n})^{\wedge, \frak{n}}$ is contained in the maximal ideal of $(R_\frak{n})^{\wedge, \frak{n}}$.

Since $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$,  we have
$R(\langle t\rangle)_\frak{n} = R_\frak{n}/\langle f\rangle$
is geometrically regular over  $k(\langle t\rangle) =K$ (Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rem.mutau\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rem.mutau}}} (2)) and hence over $k(\frak{n})$.
By Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.greg\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.greg}}} this is equivalent to $(R_\frak{n})^{\wedge, \frak{n}}/\langle f\rangle$ being geometrically regular over $k(\frak{n})$.
By Cohen (Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.cohen\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.cohen}}}) $(R_\frak{n})^{\wedge, \frak{n}} \cong k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]]$, 
$d=n-\dim K[[x]]/\frak{n}$,  and 
$k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]]/\langle f\rangle$ being regular implies
that $j(f) k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]] = k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]]$ by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.jac\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.jac}}}. By the above, this implies that $j(f) \not\subset \frak{n}$ and hence $\frak{n} \notin  V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle)$ i.e.
$V(\langle f,j(f)\rangle) \subset \operatorname{NSm}(\phi)$.


Conversely, let $\frak{n} \in V(\langle f\rangle)$, $\frak{n} \notin V(j(f))$. Then $j(f) \not\subset \frak{n}$ and by the argument above $j(f) k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]] = k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]]$, implying that
$(R_\frak{n}/\langle f\rangle)^{\wedge, \frak{n}} \cong  k(\frak{n})[[y_1,...,y_d]] / \langle f\rangle $ is geometrically regular over $k(\frak{n})$ (by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.jac\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.jac}}}) and hence $R_\frak{n}/\langle f\rangle$ is geometrically regular over $k(\frak{n})$. If $K$ is perfect, then $k(\frak{n})$ is separable algebraic over $K$ and  $R_\frak{n}/\langle f\rangle$ is geometrically regular over $K$ (by Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.reg.fieldextension\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.reg.fieldextension}}}), and hence $\frak{n} \in \operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$.
By Example 
{{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@ex.nsep\endcsname\@firstoftwo{ex.nsep}}}, $R_\frak{n}/\langle f\rangle$ need not be geometrically regular over $K$  and   
$\frak{n} \notin \operatorname{Sm}(\phi)$ if $K$ is not perfect.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- example-10: body_md -->
\label{ex.nsep}
\em{
Let $K$ be a field of characteristic $p>0$, $K'=K^{\frac{1}{p}}$ and $\alpha \in K' \smallsetminus K$. Consider 
$A=K[[t]] \to R=K[[x,y]]$, $t \mapsto f$, with
 $f=x^p+\alpha y^p$. Since $f$ is irreducible, $\frak{n} = \langle f \rangle  \subset K[[x,y]]$ is a prime ideal and since 
$\frak{n} \cap A = \langle t \rangle$, we get $k(\langle t \rangle) = K$.
The localisation of the special fiber at $\frak{n}$ is $R(\langle t \rangle)_\frak{n} = (K[[x,y]]/ \langle f \rangle)_{\langle f \rangle} = \operatorname{Quot}(K[[y]][x]/\langle f \rangle)=K((y))[x] / \langle f \rangle=K((y)) (\sqrt[p]{\alpha}y)$, which is a field (hence regular) equal to $k(\frak{n})$.
We get that
$R(\langle t \rangle)_\frak{n} \otimes_K K' = (K((y))\otimes_K K')[x] / \langle (x+ \sqrt[p]{\alpha}y)^p \rangle$ is not reduced
and hence not regular, showing that $R(\langle t \rangle)_\frak{n}$ is not geometrically regular over $K$. 
Note that $k(\frak{n})=R(\langle t \rangle)_\frak{n}$ is not separable over $K$ (\@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname[Chapter 26]{Mat86})
and not algebraic over $K$. }
<!-- end example-10 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{rm.facts} {\em
 We recall some facts from the previous chapters in our situation. Let $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$. 
 \begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em $R_\frak{p}=A_\frak{p}[x] $, 
 $ R(\frak{p}) = k(\frak{p})[x] $ and  (by Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.pctp\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.pctp}}}.1.)
$$ \hat R(\frak{p}) = (k(\frak{p})[x])^{\wedge,Ik(\frak{p})[x]}.$$
\@par\hangindent 2em  $\hat R(\frak{p})/I \hat R(\frak{p})$  is finite dimensional over $k(\frak{p})$ and a semi-local ring  with  maximal ideals 
$\bar\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1},...,\bar\frak{m}_{\frak{p},r_fp}$ (this is the starting point in Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}).\\
$\hat R(\frak{p}) \ne 0 \iff  \frak{p} \in \operatorname{Supp}_A(R/I) \iff I_\frak{p} \subsetneqq R_\frak{p}$ and the map $\operatorname{Spec} \phi: V(I) \to \operatorname{Supp}_A(R/I)$, $\frak{n} \mapsto \frak{n}\cap A$, is surjective and finite (Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.open\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.open}}}). Hence 
$$(\operatorname{Spec} \phi)^{-1}(\frak{p})\cap V(I) = \{\tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},1},...,\tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},r_\frak{p}}\}$$
for finitely many prime ideals 
$ \tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},1}, ...,\tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},r_\frak{p}}$  in $R$. 
By  Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.sem0\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.sem0}}} we have
$\tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},_i} =R\cap \bar\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}$ with $\bar\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}$ the maximal ideals of the semi-local ring 
$\hat R(\frak{p})/I \hat R(\frak{p})$
(Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}.1.).  
\@par\hangindent 2em
The ideals $\frak{n}_{\frak{p},i} = \tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},i} R_\frak{p}$ resp. 
$\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i} = \tilde \frak{n}_{\frak{p},i} \hat R(\frak{p})$ are maximal ideals in $R_\frak{p}$ resp. in $\hat R(\frak{p})$. $\hat R(\frak{p})$ is semi-local and a direct sum
\begin{equation}\label{eq.dirsum}
\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong   \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1}}\oplus\ldots\oplus \hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},r_\frak{p}}},
\quad \{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1},...,\frak{m}_{\frak{p},r_\frak{p}} \} = V(I \hat R(\frak{p})),
\end{equation}
with
$\hat R(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}} \cong
(k(\frak{p})[x]_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}})^{\wedge,\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}}
\cong k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})[[x]]$ and $k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})$ finite over $k(\frak{p})$.
\@par\hangindent 2em
 If $M$ is a finitely generated $R$-module, then  $\hat M(\frak{p})$
is finite over $k(\frak{p})$ and
$$
\begin{array}{lll}
\dim_{k(\frak{p})}  \hat M(\frak{p})&=
\sum_{i=1}^{r_\frak{p}} \dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}}\\
\smallskip
&=\sum_{i=1}^{r_\frak{p}} [k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i}):k(\frak{p})]\dim_{k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}.\\
\end{array}
$$ 
\end{enumerate}
}
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{def.mutau2}
Let $F\in R=A[x]$, $I\subset R$ an ideal with $R/I$ finite over $A$.
For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ let $\hat R(\frak{p}) = R(\frak{p})^{\wedge, I}$ be the $IR(\frak{p})$-adic completion of the fiber $R(\frak{p})$.
Define
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em the {\em Milnor number of $\hat F(\frak{p})$ along $V(I)$}  by
$$ \mu_{k(\frak{p})}^I (\hat F(\frak{p})) :=  \dim_{k(\frak{p})}  \hat R(\frak{p})/j(\hat F(\frak{p})),$$ 
\@par\hangindent 2em the {\em Tjurina number of $\hat F(\frak{p})$ along $V(I)$} by
$$ \tau_{k(\frak{p})}^I(\hat F(\frak{p})) :=  \dim_{k(\frak{p})}  \hat R(\frak{p})/\langle \hat F(\frak{p}),j(\hat F(\frak{p}))\rangle.$$ 

\@par\hangindent 2em If $I = j(F)$ we call  $\mu_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p})):=\mu^{j( F)}_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p}))$ the {\em total Milnor number} of $\hat F(\frak{p})$ and if  $I = \langle F,j(F)\rangle$, we call  $\tau_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p})):=\tau^{\langle F,j(F)\rangle}_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p}))$ the {\em total Tjurina number} of $\hat F(\frak{p})$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop.mutau}
With the notations above, let $R=A[x]$, $F\in \langle x \rangle R$ and  $I\subset R$ an ideal such that $R/I$ finite over $A$. 
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em
$\mu^I_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p}))$ and $\tau^I_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p}))$ along $V(I)$  are semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$. This holds
in particular for the total Milnor and Tjurina number.
\@par\hangindent 2em  Let  $R/j(F)$ be finite over $A$.  Then $R/j(F)$ is flat over $A$ and the total Milnor number $\mu_{k(\frak{p})} (\hat F(\frak{p}))$ is locally constant on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Set $M=R/ j(F)$ resp. $M=R/ \langle F, j(F) \rangle$. 
Then $\hat M(\frak{p})= \hat R(\frak{p})/j(\hat F(\frak{p}))$ resp.
$\hat M(\frak{p})= \hat R(\frak{p})/\langle \hat F(\frak{p}),j(\hat F(\frak{p}))\rangle$
with $\hat {}$ denoting the $I$-adic completion. 

1. The semicontinuity of $\mu^I_{k(\frak{p})}(\hat F(\frak{p}))$ resp.
$\tau^I_{k(\frak{p})}(\hat F(\frak{p}))$
follows by applying Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.scont\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.scont}}}.1. to $M$.

2. Since $R/j(F)$ is finite over $A$, for each $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ the fiber $R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p})$ is finite over $k(\frak{p})$; here $F(\frak{p})$ denotes also the image of $F$ in $R(\frak{p}) = k(\frak{p})[x_1,...,x_n]$. Since $R(\frak{p})$ is Cohen-Macaulay of dimension $n$ and $R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p}))$ has dimension 0,
the sequence 
$\partial{F(\frak{p})}/\partial {x_1},..., \partial{F({\frak{p}})}/\partial {x_n}$ is a $k(\frak{p})[x]$-regular sequence. Since $R$ is flat over $A$ it follows from \@nx\protect\@empty\@nx\csname cite \endcsname {Mat86}, Theorem 22.5 - 22.6 that $R/j(F)$ is flat over $A$.
Hence Lemma {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@lem.free\endcsname\@firstoftwo{lem.free}}} implies that 
$\dim_{k(\frak{p})} R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p}))$ is locally constant on $\operatorname{Spec} A$. Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.Afin\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.Afin}}} gives
$\dim_{k(\frak{p})} R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p}))=\dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat R(\frak{p})/j(\hat F(\frak{p})) =\mu_{k(\frak{p})}(\hat F(\frak{p}))$,  since 
$\operatorname{Supp}_R(R/j(F)) = V(j(F))$. This implies statement 2..
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- example-11: body_md -->
\label{ex.mutau} {\em
 Let $K$ be a field, $A=K[t]$ and $R=A[x,y]$. A prime ideal of $A$ is either a maximal ideal of the form $\frak{p} =\langle p(t) \rangle$, $p\in K[t]$ an irreducible (monic) polynomial, or the generic point $\frak{p} =\langle 0 \rangle$. 
 
 If $\frak{p}$ is maximal, then $k(\frak{p}) = K[t]/\langle p \rangle$ is a finite field extension of  $K$ of degree 
 $\deg(p)$. With $\bar t $ the residue class of $t$ in $K[t]/\langle p \rangle$, we have
$k(\frak{p}) \cong K(\alpha)$, $\alpha =\bar t$ a root of $p$ and
 $R(\frak{p}) = K(\alpha)[x,y]$.
  If  $\frak{p} =\langle 0 \rangle$ we get $k(\frak{p}) = K(t)$  and 
  $R(\frak{p}) = K(t)[x,y]$.
 \medskip
 
For $F\in A[x,y]$ we have
$j(F)(\frak{p}) =j(F)\cdot k(\frak{p})[x,y] \in R(\frak{p})$. 
We compute the Milnor resp. Tjurina number of $F(\frak{p})$ 
according to the formulas \eqref{eq.mutau} and we determine $\hat R(\frak{p})$. We also show that  $\mu_K (F(\frak{p}))$ may not be semicontinuous on  $\operatorname{Spec} A$, not even on the set of closed points (in contrast to $\mu_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p}))$).


 \begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em [(1)] Let $F=x^2+y^2-2tx$ and $\operatorname{char} (K) \ne 2$. Then $j(F) = \langle x-t, y\rangle$, 
$R(\frak{p})/j(F)(\frak{p})=k(\frak{p})[x,y]/ \langle x-t, y\rangle  \cong k(\frak{p})[x,y]/ \langle x, y\rangle)=k(\frak{p})$ and hence
$\mu_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) = 1$ for all $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$.
Moreover,
$$\hat R(\frak{p}) = (k(\frak{p})[x,y])^{\wedge,\langle x-t, y\rangle}  \ \cong  k(\frak{p})[x,y])^{\wedge,\langle x, y\rangle}  = k(\frak{p})[[x,y]]$$ 
is a local ring.
With the notations of Remark {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@rm.facts\endcsname\@firstoftwo{rm.facts}}}, 
we obtain $r=1$, $\bar \frak{m}_{\frak{p},1} = \langle 0\rangle$, $\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1} = \langle x-t, y\rangle \subset
\hat R(\frak{p}) $
 and $k(\frak{p}) = k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},1})$.\\

Since $\langle F , j(F) \rangle= \langle x-t, y, t^2 \rangle$ we get  $\tau_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) = 1$  if \ $\frak{p} =\langle t \rangle$
and $\tau_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) = 0$ if $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \setminus \{\langle t \rangle\}$ since $t^2$ is a unit in $\hat R(\frak{p})$.

\@par\hangindent 2em [(2)] Let $F=x^3+y^2-3tx$, $\operatorname{char} (K) \ne 2,3$. Then $j(F) = \langle x^2-t,y\rangle$ and $\langle F , j(F) \rangle= \langle y,t^2,xt,x^2-t\rangle$. For $\frak{p} =\langle p(t) \rangle$ a maximal ideal and $\alpha$ a root of $p(t)$, we get
$\hat R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p})) = K(\alpha)[x,y]/\langle x^2 -\alpha, y\rangle \cong K(\alpha)[x]/\langle x^2 -\alpha \rangle.$ This is either a local or semi-local ring, depending on $\frak{p}$.   \\
Let $\frak{p}$ be maximal.\\
- If $\sqrt{\alpha} \in  K(\alpha)$ then 
$\hat R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p}))$ is semi-local (a direct sum of two fields) with maximal ideals 
$\bar \frak{m}_{1,2}= \overline{\langle x \pm\sqrt{\alpha},y \rangle}$. Then, since $K(\alpha)[x,y]^{\wedge, \langle x \pm\sqrt{\alpha},y\rangle} \cong  K(\alpha)[[x,y]]$,
we have according to proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.sem\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.sem}}}
$$\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong K(\alpha)[[x,y]] \oplus K(\alpha)[[x,y]].$$
- If $\sqrt{\alpha} \notin  K(\alpha)$ then 
$\hat R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p}) \cong K(\sqrt{\alpha})$ is field 
and 
$$\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong K(\alpha)[x,y]^{\wedge, \langle x^2 -\alpha,y\rangle} \cong K(\sqrt{\alpha})[[x,y]].$$
Let $\frak{p} =\langle 0 \rangle$. Then 
 $\hat R(\frak{p})/j(F(\frak{p}))
\cong K(t)[x,y]/\langle x^2-t,y\rangle\cong K(\sqrt{t})$ and hence $\hat R(\frak{p}) \cong K(\sqrt{t})[[x,y]].$\\
We get $\mu_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) = 2$ for all $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$
and (as in (1))  $\tau_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) = 2$  if \ $\frak{p} =\langle t \rangle$
and $\tau_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) = 0$ if $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A \setminus \{\langle t \rangle\}$.
\@par\hangindent 2em [(3)] For $\frak{p}=\langle p(t)\rangle$ a maximal ideal we get
$\mu_K (F(\frak{p})) =\deg(p)\cdot \mu_{k(\frak{p})} (F(\frak{p})) <\infty$,
but since $K(t)$ is a infinite extension of $K$ we get $\mu_K (F(\langle 0 \rangle))= \infty$ if $\frak{p} = \langle 0 \rangle$.
Hence $\mu_K(F(\frak{p}))$ is not semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} K[t]$.\\
For example, let $F=x^3+y^2-3tx \in \mathbbm{Q}[t,x,y]$. If $\frak{p} =\langle t-c \rangle$, $c \in \mathbbm{Q}$, then $\mu_{\mathbbm{Q}} (F(\frak{p})) =2$.
If $\frak{p}_n =\langle t^2-1/n \rangle$ and $n$ not a square, then $\mu_{\mathbbm{Q}} (F(\frak{p}_n)) =4$ and $\frak{p}_n$ is arbitrary close to $\langle t\rangle$ for large $n$. This shows that  $\mu_{\mathbbm{Q}} (F(\frak{p}))$ is not semicontinuous on the set of closed points of
$\operatorname{Spec} \mathbbm{Q}[t]$. In fact, $\mu_{\mathbbm{Q}} (F(\frak{p}))$ can be arbitrary big for $\frak{p}$ close to $\langle t\rangle$.
\end{enumerate}
}
<!-- end example-11 -->
<!-- corollary-7: body_md -->
\label{cor.mutau}
Let $A$ be a Noetherian ring, $R=A[x]$ or $R=A[[x]]$, $F \in R$, and $I\subset R$ the ideal of a section $\sigma$ of $A\to R$.
 For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ denote by $\hat F(\frak{p})$
the image of $F$ in $k(\frak{p})[[x]]$.
Then the Milnor and the Tjurina number along the section $\sigma$ 
$$
\begin{array}{lll}
\mu_{k(\frak{p})}  (\hat F(\frak{p})) &=& \dim_{k(\frak{p})}  k(\frak{p})[[x]]/ j(\hat F(\frak{p}))
\text{ and }\\
\tau_{k(\frak{p})}  (\hat F(\frak{p})) &=& \dim_{k(\frak{p})}  k(\frak{p})[[x]]/ \langle \hat F(\frak{p}), j(\hat F(\frak{p}))\rangle
\end{array}
 $$
are semicontinuous on $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
<!-- end corollary-7 -->
<!-- corollary-8: body_md -->
\label{cor.mutau2}
With the assumptions of Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.mutau\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.mutau}}} 
 and $\frak{p}$ a  {\em maximal ideal} of $A$
the following holds.
For $\frak{q} \in \operatorname{Spec} A_i$ the ring $\hat Q_i(\frak{q})$ is semi-local with maximal ideals, say, $\frak{n}_{i,1},...,\frak{n}_{i,t}$,
and $\hat M_{Q_i} := M\hat \otimes_R Q_i$ satisfies $\hat M_{Q_i} (\frak{p}) = \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}$. 
Then the semicontinuity of
 $\dim_{k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i}$
 means
$$\dim_{k(\frak{p})} \hat M(\frak{p})_{\frak{m}_i} \geq
\sum_{j=1}^t \dim_{k(\frak{q})} \hat M_{Q_i}(\frak{q})_{\frak{n}_{i,j}}
 \ \text {for all } \frak{q} \in U_i.$$
In particular,
$$ 
\begin{array}{lll}
 \mu^I_{k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})} (\hat F(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})) & \geq &
\sum_{j=1}^t [k(\frak{q}):k(\frak{n}_{\frak{q},j})]\mu_{k(\frak{n}_{\frak{q},j})}(\hat F(\frak{n}_{\frak{q},j})),\\
\tau^I_{k(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})} (\hat F(\frak{m}_{\frak{p},i})) &\geq &
\sum_{j=1}^t [k(\frak{q}):k(\frak{n}_{\frak{q},j})]\tau_{k(\frak{n}_{\frak{q},j})}(\hat F(\frak{n}_{\frak{q},j})). 
\end{array}
$$
<!-- end corollary-8 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
\label{def.tau}
Let $f_1, \ldots, f_m$ be generators of $J$. We set
$$
\begin{array}{lll}
T_{J/K} &:= & K[[x]]^m\big/J\cdot K[[x]]^m+\langle {\partial f}/{\partial x_1}, \ldots, {\partial f}/{\partial x_n}\rangle\cdot K[[x]].\\
\tau_K(J) &:= &\dim_K T_{J/K},
\end{array}
$$
and call $T_{J/K}$ the {\em Tjurina module of $J$ w.r.t. $K$} and
$\tau_K(J)$ the {\em Tjurina number of $J$ w.r.t. $K$}.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
{\em
The notation $T_{J/K}$ is a bit misleading, as $T_{J/K}$ depends not just on $J$ but also on the chosen generators.
However, $T_{J/K}$ is unique up to isomorphism and 
$\tau_K(J)$ is an invariant of $K[[x]]/J$.

To see this,  let $g_1, \ldots, g_k$ be another  set of generators of $J$. W.l.o.g.  let $m \ge k$ and set $g_j=0$ for $j=k+1,...,m$. 
By Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.contact\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.contact}}} below there exists an invertible matrix $U \in GL_m(K[[x]])$ such that 
$(f_1,...,f_m) = (g_1,...,g_m)\cdot U$. Then $U$ induces an isomorphism between $T_{J/K}$ defined with $f_1,...,f_m$ and $T_{J/K}$ defined with $g_1,...,g_m$.
}
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop.contact}
	Let $I$ and $J$ be proper ideals of $K[[x]]$ given by the same number of $m$ generators $\{f_1,...,f_m\}$ and
$\{g_1,...,g_m\}$.
	Then the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em The analytic algebras $K[[x]]/I$ and $K[[x]]/J$ are isomorphic.
\@par\hangindent 2em  There is an automorphism $\phi\in Aut(K[[x]])$ such that $\phi(I)=J$.
\@par\hangindent 2em There are  $\phi\in Aut(K[[x]])$ and $U\in GL(m,K[[x]])$ such that $(f_1,...,f_m) = (\phi(g_1),...,\phi(g_m))\cdot U$.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, if $I=J$, then $\phi$ can be chosen as the identity.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm.GPh19}
Let $J \subset  K[[x]]$ be a proper ideal. Then the following are equivalent:
\smallskip

(i) $\tau_K(J) <\infty$.	
\smallskip

(ii)  $K[[x]]/J$ is an ICIS. 
\smallskip
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop.TI} 
Let $A$ be Noetherian, $R=A[x]$ and   
$I\subset R$ an ideal such that $R/I$ is finite over $A$. Let $F_1, \ldots, F_m \in R$ and  $J \subset  R$ the ideal generated by  $F_1,\ldots, F_m $.  Let $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ and $\hat J(\frak{p}) \subset \hat R(\frak{p})$  the ideal generated by $F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,F_m(\frak{p}) \in \hat R(\frak{p})$.
Then $\frak{p}$ has an open neighbourhood $U \subset \operatorname{Spec} A$ such that   
$$\tau_{k(\frak{p})}({\hat J(\frak{p})})\geq  \tau_{k(\frak{q})}(\hat J(\frak{q})) \text{ for all } \frak{q} \in U.$$
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
The finitely generated $R$-module
$T_{J/A}$ satisfies $\hat T_{J(\frak{p})/k(\frak{p})}=(T_{J/A}\otimes_Ak(\frak{p}))^\wedge$. The statement follows from Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.scont\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.scont}}}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop.icis}
Let $A$ be a Noetherian ring, $R=A[x]$ and  $I \subset R$ an ideal  such that $ R/I R$ is finite over $A$.
Let $J\subset R$ be an ideal generated by 
 $F_1,\ldots, F_m \in R$, such that $ R/J R$ is $A$-flat.
 For $\frak{p} \in \operatorname{Spec} A$ denote  by $\hat J(\frak{p}) \subset  \hat R(\frak{p})$  the ideal generated by $\hat F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{p})$.

\begin{enumerate}
\@par\hangindent 2em  If  $\hat I(\frak{p})$ is a complete intersection, then $\hat I(\frak{q})$ is a complete intersection for $\frak{q}$ in an open neighbourhood of $\frak{p}$ in $\operatorname{Spec} A$. 
\@par\hangindent 2em  Assume that $\hat I(\frak{p})$ is an ICIS.
Then  $\hat I(\frak{q})$ is an ICIS with Tjurina numbers
$\tau_{k(\frak{p})}(\hat I(\frak{p})) \geq \tau_{k(\frak{q})}(\hat I(\frak{q}))$ for $\frak{q}$ in an open neighbourhood of $\frak{p}$ in $\operatorname{Spec} A$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
1. Since $\hat I(\frak{p})$ is a complete intersection, we may assume that $\hat F_1(\frak{p}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{p})$ is a $\hat R(\frak{p})$-sequence. By Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.reg\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.reg}}} (iii), applied to $M=R$ (the assumptions are fullfilled), 
  $\hat F_1(\frak{q}),\ldots,\hat F_m(\frak{q})$ is a $\hat R(\frak{q})$-sequence, hence $\hat I(\frak{q})$ is a complete intersection, for $\frak{q}$ in an open neighbourhood of $\frak{p}$ in $\operatorname{Spec} A$.\\
2. follows from Proposition {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@prop.TI\endcsname\@firstoftwo{prop.TI}}} since  for $\hat J(\frak{q}) \subset \hat R(\frak{q})]$ a complete intersection  
$\tau_{k(\frak{q})}(\hat I(\frak{q}))$ is the Tjurina number of $\hat J(\frak{q})$.
 Moreover, $\hat I(\frak{q})$ is am ICIS by Theorem {{\rm\expandafter\@setref\csname r@thm.GPh19\endcsname\@firstoftwo{thm.GPh19}}}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
Let us mention, that the Milnor number $\mu(X)$ for a complex analytic  ICIS $X$ is defined topologically as the middle Betti number of the Milnor fiber of a smoothing. With this definition it can  be proved topologically
 that the Milnor number is semicontinuous under deformations.
 However, the topological definition cannot be used for arbitrary fields.
<!-- end head-1 -->
